The picker doubles as the "can't find it? add it" path: when the search has no exact key match it offers create-custom (`createCustomExercise`), merging the new owner entry into the composer's local catalogue so it binds without a server reload. Browse self-hides when there's no catalogue (offline / signed-out), keeping the free-text path the always-works baseline (§176's additive contract). The picker UI does **not** change PR keying (still normalised name) or the offline write path.
- **Still not built**: muscle-group analytics off `category`, public/shared customs, binding `exercise_id` from a routine step. Deferred.

## 177. The lifecycle drip selects its cohorts in SQL and rides the digest's rails as a new `lifecycle_drip` kind — one mechanism, three templates, its own opt-in, a stream-scoped unsubscribe

**Decided (2026-06-20, migration `20270223_001`).** The "lifecycle drip (re-engagement / onboarding / streak nudges)" engagement item is built as the third engagement stream on the EXACT rails the weekly digest established (`§ 174`), not a bespoke path:
- **A new `lifecycle_drip` jobs.kind carrying `{user_id, template}`**, three templates under one mechanism (`drip_onboarding`, `drip_reengagement`, `drip_streak`) — mirroring how `lifecycle_email` carries welcome / pro_welcome / payment_failed. The worker handler (`handler_lifecycle_drip.go`) is the digest handler's sibling: nil-sender skip, opt-in gate, suppression hard-block, render, send.
- **Cohort selection lives in the `enqueue_lifecycle_drip()` SECURITY DEFINER SQL function**, not the Go handler — the durable split. Set-based windowed queries (onboarding = opted-in account 2–6 days old with no run; re-engagement = a run >30 d ago but no row in the cross-modal `activities` view in 30 d; streak = ran the last two calendar days but not yet today) decide who gets which template and write it into the payload; the handler just honours the gate and sends the named template. A daily 09:00-UTC pg_cron fires it, dedupe-safe per `(user_id, template)`.
- **A SEPARATE opt-IN pref `email_lifecycle_drip`** (default off), never folded into `email_notifications` (transactional) NOR `email_weekly_digest` (the other engagement stream) — opting into one engagement stream is not consent to another (CAN-SPAM + GDPR/ePrivacy). Gated in BOTH the enqueue SQL and the handler (defence in depth).
- **The unsubscribe token + endpoint are generalised to be stream-aware**, rather than copied: `digesttoken.Mint/Verify` take a `stream` arg (the scope namespaces the MAC so one stream's link can't unsubscribe another), and `internal/unsubscribe` mounts `/unsubscribe/weekly-digest` AND `/unsubscribe/lifecycle-drip` off the ONE shared `WEEKLY_DIGEST_UNSUB_SECRET`. The per-stream pref flip is independent, but the `email_suppressions` row a suppression inserts blocks EVERY stream to that address (the safe default — one "stop emailing me" stops all marketing).
- **Why SQL-side cohort selection + fail-closed gate** (the tradeoff): the alternative — a Go builder that fetches candidates and computes cohorts in-process — duplicates the windowing the DB does natively, fans out N reads per candidate, and drifts from the data. Pushing selection into one `enqueue_*()` function (the precedent every other cron uses) keeps it one set-based pass and makes the cron the operator-flippable surface. Per `CLAUDE.md` "compliance gates prod, not code": the whole path ships, fail-closed on the unset SMTP credential (jobs drain to `done` without `SMTP_HOST`), with CISO/counsel sign-off recorded as a pre-deploy checklist item — the cron is harmless no-op churn until SMTP is provisioned.

## 178. The ChronoTrack results-import leg is a `provider:'chronotrack'` branch on the existing `race-results-import` EF, built fail-closed behind its own `CHRONOTRACK_*` credentials and reusing the RunSignUp mapper

**Decided (2026-06-20).** ChronoTrack — one of the deferred per-site scrapers from `§ 168` — is built as a third provider leg on the SAME `race-results-import` Edge Function, mirroring the shipped RunSignUp leg exactly rather than introducing a new function or a bespoke shape:
- **One EF, a new `provider === 'chronotrack'` branch.** It calls the ChronoTrack Live (CTLive) REST results endpoint server-side (`chronoTrackResultsUrl` in `lib.ts`), extracts the nested `event_results[]` envelope (`extractChronoTrackResults`, capped at `MAX_RESULTS_ROWS`, tolerating a top-level `results[]`), and maps each finisher via `mapChronoTrackResult`. That mapper **delegates to `mapRunSignUpResult`** so the run row shape, the chip→gun time fallback, the `external_id=race:{name}:{date}:{bib}`, the per-user dedup, and the owner-only race metadata keys are byte-for-byte identical across providers — only the CTLive field names (`results_bib`/`results_time`/`results_gun_time`/`results_rank`/`results_division_rank`/`results_division`) differ, and they're translated in one place.
- **Fail-closed behind three credentials.** The leg is gated on `CHRONOTRACK_CLIENT_ID` + `CHRONOTRACK_USER_ID` + `CHRONOTRACK_PASSWORD` (the CTLive `client_id` + the account `user_id`/`user_pass` pair, the natural analogue of RunSignUp's `_API_KEY`/`_API_SECRET`). Any one unset → the pure `chronoTrackConfigured(getEnv)` predicate is false → the branch returns `503 provider_not_configured` and writes nothing. The dev/CI default is all-unset, so the leg is inert until an operator provisions the trio — the genuine-external-blocker case of the compliance-gates-prod rule (build the whole path now, default the gate off). Provisioning the three creds is recorded as the only remaining go-live step.
- **A `probe:true` mode on the same EF** reports provider availability without needing a listing, so the Settings → Integrations ChronoTrack card (web + mobile twin) can disable itself with the same "unavailable until provisioned" explainer the RunSignUp card shows. RunSignUp probes via `race-listings-sync`; ChronoTrack has no sync EF, so the probe rides the import EF — a smaller seam than a second function.
- **Why not a per-site HTML scraper** (the `§ 168` deferral): CTLive is an authenticated JSON API, so the durable path is the same server-side API call + dedup the RunSignUp leg uses, not the brittle/abusive HTML crawl the deferral warned against. RaceResult / UltraSignup stay deferred precisely because they lack an equivalently clean API surface; manual paste remains their non-API path.
- **Not built**: the opt-in toggle UI (web Settings checkbox + mobile switch) for the engagement streams — the pref is read server-side today; a per-recipient send cap / frequency cap across the two streams; analytics on drip open/convert. Deferred.

## 178. Route-photo server-side thumbnails + EXIF strip reuse the run-photo worker shape — a `route_photo_process` kind with bucket-aware backend methods, not a copied handler

**Decided (2026-06-20, migration `20270224_001`).** The deferred half of roadmap row 8 (server-side thumbnails + an EXIF worker for route photos) ships as the exact sibling of the run-photo `photo_process` path, not a parallel implementation:

- **A new `route_photo_process` jobs.kind** carrying the identical `{photo_id, storage_path, owner_id}` payload as `photo_process` (the `PhotoProcessPayload` struct is shared — same shape, same meaning). The worker's `handleRoutePhotoProcess` is the run handler's twin: download → strip JPEG APP1/COM → 512w thumbnail → PATCH `route_photos.thumb_512_path`. Non-JPEG is a no-op; bad payload / empty path are permanent; storage 5xx is transient.
- **Storage access is refactored to bucket-aware private helpers** (`downloadFromBucket` / `uploadToBucket` / `updateThumb512Path` on `SupabaseClient`), and BOTH the run-photo and the new route-photo public methods delegate to them. The run methods hardcoded the `run-photos` bucket; rather than copy three methods with a different bucket literal, the literal becomes a parameter so there is one transport path. The `Backend` interface gains `DownloadRoutePhoto` / `UploadRoutePhoto` / `UpdateRoutePhotoThumb512Path`.
- **Two enqueue triggers** cover the two upload shapes the clients already use: an AFTER INSERT (web uploads the object first, then inserts the row with the final `storage_path`) and an AFTER UPDATE OF `storage_path` (mobile inserts a placeholder empty-path row, uploads, then PATCHes the real path). The placeholder insert is skipped; the service-role thumb PATCH never re-enqueues (it doesn't change `storage_path`).
- **Defence in depth, not a behaviour change for clients.** The web + mobile route-photo galleries were already built forward-compatible — they select `thumb_512_path`, sign it, and prefer it (`p.thumbUrl ?? p.url` / `_galleryUrl`) — and the clients already strip EXIF client-side before upload. This change populates the column the clients were waiting on and adds the server-side re-strip so a route's photos don't depend solely on a well-behaved client. No client code changed.
- **Why mirror the run path rather than generalise into one handler taking a bucket+table arg** (the tradeoff): the two handlers are ~30 lines of straight-line glue and the per-kind dispatch + the per-kind enqueue trigger already make them distinct units; collapsing them into one parameterised handler would couple two job kinds' error semantics and obscure the dispatch. The DRY win that matters — the HTTP transport — lives in the shared `SupabaseClient` helpers, not the handler. Pinned by 8 Go handler tests + the pgtap `route_photos_enqueue_process_test.sql` (6 assertions) + the allowlist test.
## 179. Learn-guide prose localizes via a `<slug>.<locale>.md` lookup with field-by-field English fallback — applied uniformly to body, H1, and listing cards; the English copy stays the prerendered canonical

**Decided (2026-06-20).** The `/learn` surface shipped (`§ 161`) with six-locale chrome but English-only prose and a half-wired resolver. The prose-localization mechanism is now complete on the existing repo-committed-MDsvex foundation, not on a DB-backed translation store: a guide's translation is just another committed file, `apps/web/src/lib/learn/guides/<slug>.<locale>.md`, indexed by the same `import.meta.glob` as the English source. Three pure resolvers in `guides.ts` close it: `getGuide(slug, locale)` (localized body, else English), `localizedGuideMeta(slug, locale)` (localized title/description **falling back field-by-field** to the English frontmatter), and `isEnglishFallback(slug, locale)` (drives the "this guide is in English" notice). The non-obvious calls:
- **Localize the LISTING, not just the article.** The hub + category cards build off the English index (one card per slug), so localizing only the article body would show a non-English visitor an English card title above a localized body a click away. `GuideCard` + the article H1 both re-resolve through `localizedGuideMeta`, so the listing → body reads consistently in the active locale and degrades to English as one unit.
- **The English body stays the prerendered, canonical copy.** The site is `adapter-static`; `entries()` bakes the English guide into `build/learn/<slug>.html` with English `<head>` meta (canonical URL, OG, `Article` JSON-LD). The localized body + title are lazy-resolved **client-side** after `initLocale` swaps the active locale — there is one indexed URL per guide (no `/de/learn/...` variants, no `hreflang` fan-out), which is the right SEO posture for an acquisition surface whose canonical language is English.
- **Localized frontmatter must agree with its English sibling on the structural fields.** `slug`/`category`/`order`/`cta.feature` are identical to the source (only `title`/`description` translate); `guides.test.ts` fails the build on drift, because the card's route + section come off the English index and a mis-filed localized file would link a card under the wrong category. The resolver keys off the filename suffix, which must be a supported non-default locale with an English source to fall back to — also guarded.
- **Why repo-committed files over a translation CMS / message-catalogue keys** (the tradeoff): guide prose is long-form evergreen Markdown, not UI strings — putting it in the `m()` catalogue would bloat every locale chunk and lose the MDsvex authoring/review-in-PR ergonomics that `§ 161` chose deliberately; a CMS reintroduces the runtime + attack surface `§ 161` rejected. A per-locale sibling file keeps translations version-controlled, prerender-compatible, and addable without touching code. The cost is that a new translation is a code change + deploy, accepted as consistent with `§ 161`.
- **Shipped state:** `road-running-101` is fully translated into all six locales as the proof + template; the other seven guides stay English and the resolver falls back. Authoring the remaining translations is content work with no further mechanism change.

## 180. Nutrition barcode scan is mobile-only via `mobile_scanner` (BSD-3) over the shared Open Food Facts lookup, wrapped as an L4 auxiliary path

The v1.1 nutrition fast-path — scan an EAN/UPC with the phone camera instead of typing a search — is **mobile-only** (web has no camera-record surface, decisions §24) and rides the **same** Open Food Facts lookup the search path already uses. The scan resolves a raw barcode to a product via OFF's product-by-barcode endpoint (`/api/v2/product/{code}.json`, the `{status, product}` single-object shape), distinct from the search endpoint's `{products: [...]}` array, so it gets its own pure parser `parseOffProduct` + `lookupBarcode` + `normaliseBarcode` (strip to digits) in the `food_search` TS↔Dart parity pair. The web side carries the same three helpers purely for lockstep — nothing on web calls them.

- **Package: `mobile_scanner` ^7.2.0, BSD-3-Clause.** Chosen for the permissive licence (same reasoning as `flutter_reactive_ble` over `flutter_blue_plus`, decisions §… BLE note) and because it ships the scanner with the platform engine (CameraX/ML Kit on Android, AVFoundation/Apple Vision on iOS) — no separate ML download, no extra entitlement beyond the camera permission. Added identically to both twin pubspecs (only `name`/`description` may differ).
- **L4 layered resilience.** The whole scan → lookup path in `nutrition_log_sheet.dart` is wrapped in its own try/catch and degrades to the always-present search / manual-entry fallback on any failure: a cancelled scan is a no-op, a no-match shows a distinct "no product found" message, a network/parse failure shows a distinct "scan failed" message — none of them can break manual logging. Camera-permission denial surfaces an inline message + an Open-settings affordance (`permission_handler.openAppSettings`) rather than a dead black frame.
- **Native permissions.** Android `CAMERA` is declared with `uses-feature android:required="false"` so a camera-less device still installs (the scan affordance degrades). iOS `NSCameraUsageDescription` is added to `Info.plist` and pinned by the `iOS Info.plist` group in `architecture_guards_test.dart`.
- **Testing.** The camera itself isn't unit-testable; the lookup-on-scan handler is, via an injectable `BarcodeScanner` seam on the sheet (returns a canned raw barcode) + the existing `FoodFetcher` seam — match → portion dialog, no-match → message, failure → distinct message, cancel → no-op. The pure barcode helpers are covered by the `food_search` parity-pair tests on both platforms.
- **Sub-processor note (carry-over):** barcode lookups hit the same Open Food Facts host already listed for search; no new outbound data flow. The privacy-disclosure carry-over (iOS Privacy Nutrition Label / Play Data Safety — camera usage + OFF as a sub-processor) joins the existing nutrition pre-real-user-launch checklist.

## 181. Per-shoe wear-pattern logging is its own owner-only `gear_wear_logs` table, not more keys on `gear` — the qualitative companion to the distance-based wear classifier

**Decided (2026-06-20, migration `20270225_001`).** The gear-tracking roadmap item (§7) left two "future" gaps: multi-pair rotation tagging and per-shoe wear-pattern logging. The latter is the higher-value, genuinely-unbuilt piece (rotation tagging largely overlaps the existing `is_default` "current pair" auto-tag concept), so it ships first:
- **A thin `gear_wear_logs` table** (`gear_id`, `owner_id`, `logged_on` date, optional `area` ∈ outsole/midsole/upper/other, free-text `note`), one row per dated observation, NOT new columns on `gear`. Shoes don't wear linearly — "outsole lugs gone at 380 km", "midsole feels dead" — so the model is a time-series of notes, which the single static `gear.notes` field can't be. It complements (does not replace) the `gear_wear` distance classifier: the progress bar says *how far*, the wear log says *what you noticed*.
- **Owner-only RLS end to end, with NO public-visibility path.** Unlike `run_gear` (whose SELECT follows the parent run so a public run can show its gear chip), wear notes are private inventory metadata and never leak — the `public_run_gear` RPC already projects only id/kind/name/brand, and wear logs aren't in it. INSERT is gated on owning BOTH the log row (`owner_id = auth.uid()`) AND the parent `gear` (the same double-gate `run_gear` uses), so a user can't hang a log off another user's gear. Rows cascade on parent-gear delete.
- **Web is canonical full-CRUD inline** on the `/settings/gear` edit modal; **mobile mirrors as online-only add/list/delete** from the gear edit form, deliberately OUTSIDE the offline `LocalGearStore` pipeline — exactly the call the gear-backfill sheet made (decisions §73 covers the offline gear store; backfill + wear logs are the two online-only gear sub-flows). A wear log is a low-frequency, reflective action; the extra offline-sync plumbing (a second per-row sync-state machine) isn't worth it for v1. If a runner is offline when they want to jot a note, the gear edit form's existing `notes` field still works.
- **Not built**: multi-pair rotation tagging (the other §7 future gap — deferred, overlaps `is_default`); offline-queued wear logs; surfacing the wear log on the run-detail gear chip (it's owner-private, the chip is public). Barcode import stays deferred.

## 182. The public gym-routine library is a flag on the routine itself (`is_public_template`), not a separate template row — publishing flips a bit, adoption deep-copies, and a CHECK keeps it disjoint from club templates

The roadmap's "Workout templates" mid-tier item had two halves: build-your-own (shipped with the gym routine engine P1, `20270101_001`) and a public, adoptable library. The gym-programming v1 RLS note (`gym_programming.md`) explicitly deferred the public-read branch with one condition: ship it "in the *same* migration that ships the browse UI", because a public-read policy with no browse surface lets any authenticated user enumerate every public routine's planned sets through the REST API (a `public-rows` leak). Migration `20270226_001` satisfies that condition — the public-read RLS, the clone branch, and the `/gym/routines/library` browse UI land together.

**Two shapes were available, mirroring the two sibling libraries.** The public PLAN library (`clone_public_plan`, `20270126_001`) reuses the template plumbing: a public plan is `is_public_template = true` AND a separate `is_template` row, and publishing copies the source plan into a new template row (because a plan carries the publisher's private VDOT, which must be stripped). The club gym-routine templates (`20270109_001`) deep-copy a personal routine into a *new* club-owned routine on publish. For the public gym-routine library we chose the **flag-on-the-routine** shape instead: `set_gym_routine_public(routine_id, public)` flips `is_public_template` on the routine the caller already owns — no copy, the routine IS the template. This is correct here because a gym routine carries **no private fitness data** — its target loads ARE the published prescription (the whole point of sharing a 5/3/1 template) — so there is nothing to strip, and a separate template row would only create a sync burden (edits to the source wouldn't reach the published copy). Adoption still deep-copies (`clone_gym_routine_template` gained a third authorisation branch) into a personal, club-less, non-public routine, so a cloner owns an independent copy.

**A CHECK keeps the two visibility axes disjoint.** `gym_routines_public_not_club` enforces `is_public_template ⇒ club_id is null`: a routine is either club-owned (member-gated) or public (anyone-gated) or private, never both. This mirrors the plan library's strict club-vs-public separation and means the RLS branches (author-only base, club-member read, public read) OR together cleanly with no overlap to reason about. `set_gym_routine_public` also refuses a club-owned routine server-side, so the CHECK can't be tripped through the toggle.

The publish/unpublish toggle is author-gated in a SECURITY DEFINER RPC rather than left to the existing author-UPDATE RLS — the RLS would already permit the flip, but a dedicated RPC keeps the toggle explicit + auditable, enforces author-only + not-club-owned in one place, and gives both clients a single call instead of a bare column write through PostgREST.

## 183. Gear "rotations" are a thin owner-only `gear_rotations` + `gear_rotation_members` many-to-many, distinct from and additive to the single `is_default` current pair

The roadmap's gear §7 listed two "Future" gaps; per-shoe wear logging shipped as §181, leaving multi-pair "rotation" tagging. The roadmap note (and the gear-default migration `20260901_001`) flagged that rotation tagging "largely overlaps `is_default`". It does not, and conflating them would lose information: `is_default` marks exactly **one** current pair per `(owner, kind)` (a partial-unique index enforces it) and exists to **auto-tag new runs** via the `runs`-insert trigger. A rotation is a **named, many-to-many grouping** a runner organises gear into ("Daily trainers", "Race day") — one shoe can be in several rotations, a rotation holds several shoes, and assigning a rotation changes **nothing** about auto-tagging. So rotations are a separate feature built additively, not folded into the default flag.

**Shape (`20270227_001`):** a thin `gear_rotations` (id, owner_id, name) + a `gear_rotation_members` join (rotation_id, gear_id, PK on the pair), both owner-scoped exactly like `gear`. RLS mirrors the established pattern: 4 owner-only policies on the rotation, and the **run_gear / gear_wear_logs double-gate** on the join — a member INSERT must own BOTH the rotation AND the parent gear, so a user can't slip another user's gear into their rotation, nor add gear to a rotation they don't own. Unlike `run_gear` there is **no public-visibility path** — a rotation is private inventory organisation, never projected onto a public run, so `public_run_gear` is untouched. Deleting a rotation cascades its members; deleting a gear cascades its memberships (the gear stays where it's the only referent — a rotation is just a grouping).

**Web is canonical** (`/settings/gear`): manage rotations + a member-checkbox modal + a filter dropdown that groups the gear list by rotation. **Mobile mirrors online-only** — a `GearRotationsScreen` reached from the gear AppBar, reading/writing rotations through the api while reading gear rows from `LocalGearStore` for the member picker. Rotations deliberately sit **outside** the offline `LocalGearStore` (the same precedent as the wear-log + gear-backfill sub-flows, §73): a rotation is organisation, not a record that must survive offline, so the offline-sync machinery isn't worth the plumbing. The `GearRotationWithMembers` shape (rotation + member ids) is computed client-side from two owner-scoped reads on both platforms; a runner has a handful of rotations, so a join-view wasn't warranted.
## 184. Club photos are a `club_photos` gallery re-keying the route-photo shape to club membership — member-upload, owner-or-admin delete, a club-visibility SELECT gate, and a `club_photo_process` worker

**Decided (2026-06-20, migration `20270301_001`).** The deferred "club-photo features" half of "Photos on runs and routes" (roadmap backlog row 8) is built as a third photo surface on the exact shape `run_photos` + `route_photos` established, with the permission gates re-keyed from row-ownership to club membership:

- **`club_photos` table + a PRIVATE `club-photos` Storage bucket**, mirroring `route_photos` (20270114_001): `{owner_id}/{photo_id}.{ext}` paths, owner-prefix shape CHECKs, the no-blank-clear + service-role-only-`thumb_512_path` UPDATE triggers, clients read via `createSignedUrl`. The bucket is private from the start (the run-photos public-CDN-bypass lesson).
- **The permission gates differ from route_photos by design.** SELECT is gated on club *visibility* (`clubs.is_public OR owner OR private.is_club_member`), not route visibility — a public club's gallery is readable by anyone (incl. anon), a private club's only by active members / the owner. INSERT requires `owner_id = auth.uid() AND private.is_club_member(club_id)` — *any active member* contributes, not just the club owner (the whole point of a club gallery). DELETE is photo-owner OR `private.is_club_admin(club_id)` — a member removes their own, an owner/admin moderates anyone's. Caption UPDATE stays photo-owner-only. `private.is_club_member`/`is_club_admin` are the `active`-only SECURITY DEFINER oracles (20261120_001), so a pending join request grants neither read nor write — fail-closed, like every other club surface.
- **A `club_photo_process` worker** (`apps/job_worker` `handleClubPhotoProcess`) is the `photo_process` sibling against the club bucket/table: download → re-strip JPEG EXIF (defence in depth over the client-side `stripExifFromFile`/`stripJpegExif`) → 512w thumbnail → service-role PATCH of `thumb_512_path`. Enqueued by an AFTER INSERT trigger plus an AFTER UPDATE OF `storage_path` trigger that catches the mobile insert-then-PATCH placeholder path. Three-file rule honoured: the `jobs.kind` CHECK widen + the Go dispatch case + the pgtap kind-acceptance assertion land together. `club_photos` is wired into the GDPR Art 20 export spec (owner_id-keyed, like route_photos).
- **Surfaces**: a Photos tab on the web club detail page (`ClubPhotos.svelte`) + the mobile club detail screen (`club_photos.dart`, byte-identical twin); upload gated on membership, delete on owner-or-admin, non-members of a public club see it read-only.
- **Prod gate**: this is a privacy-boundary surface (user uploads + club visibility). The RLS is fail-closed and pgtap-pinned, but a privacy audit (`/audit/privacy-zones` reasoning + `/audit/storage`) is a pre-prod deploy gate, recorded as a checklist item per `CLAUDE.md` "compliance gates prod, not code" — the code ships behind no flag because the gate is the RLS itself.
- **ADR number**: §184, assigned at merge time (the round-2 club-photos branch authored this as a deliberately-high placeholder to avoid collision with sibling workers).

## 185. The UltraSignup race-results leg follows the RunSignUp pattern exactly — a per-provider fail-closed gate on its own credential, the same `lib.ts` URL-pattern dispatch, no schema change

**Decided (2026-06-20).** The race-calendar (`§ deferred backlog #10`, migration `20270214_001`) shipped with parkrun + RunSignUp legs and left ChronoTrack / UltraSignup / RaceResult as scoped follow-ups. The UltraSignup leg ships now as the trail/ultra-results provider, built as the precise sibling of the RunSignUp leg rather than a parallel path:

- **No schema change.** `RaceProvider` already admitted `ultrasignup` (the CHECK + TS union shipped with the calendar), so the leg is pure function + Edge Function code: a `extractUltraSignUpResults` parser in `race-results-import/lib.ts` next to the RunSignUp parser, dispatched by the existing provider switch, and the `race-listings-sync` per-provider probe gate.
- **Its own fail-closed credential gate.** `ULTRASIGNUP_API_KEY` / `ULTRASIGNUP_API_SECRET` are a SEPARATE pair from `RUNSIGNUP_*` — unset → that leg returns `503 provider_not_configured` and the Settings integrations card shows the unavailable explainer, exactly like RunSignUp. parkrun + manual-paste keep working regardless. Provisioning the credential is the only go-live step; the whole code path is built + fixture-tested (20 lib tests) behind the gate, per `CLAUDE.md` "compliance/credentials gate prod, not code".
- **Why a per-provider gate, not one shared race-import flag**: a partner credential is provider-scoped (UltraSignup approval is independent of RunSignUp's), so one provider being unconfigured must not 503 the others. The gate is keyed on each provider's own env pair; the `RACE_IMPORT_USER_AGENT` politeness identifier is shared.
- **Still deferred**: ChronoTrack + RaceResult per-site scrapers (the fragility cost — structured paste remains the durable path for non-API sites).

## 186. The vert challenge metric first-classes total ascent as `runs.elevation_gain_m`, not a summed jsonb key — the column is the additive queryable source the aggregate groups on, the metadata key stays for the existing readers

**Decided (2026-06-20, migration `20270302_001`).** Challenges shipped (`20270209_001` + `20270210_001`) with `distance`/`duration`/`activity_count`/`streak_days`; `vert` was deferred because total elevation gain had no first-class per-run column — it lived only in `runs.metadata.elevation_m`, populated for some imports (Strava/Garmin) and absent for app-recorded runs. The challenge aggregate (`challenge_leaderboard` / `recompute_challenge_completion`) sums BASE `runs` columns directly (`sum(r.distance_m)` etc.), so a `vert` board summing a jsonb key would have undercounted and couldn't be expressed cleanly in the GROUP-BY.

- **`runs.elevation_gain_m numeric` (nullable)** is the first-class total ascent, promoted exactly the way `activity_type`/`is_dnf` were (`20261207_001`). Backfilled from `metadata.elevation_m` for every run carrying it; a partial index `runs_elevation_gain (user_id, started_at) where elevation_gain_m is not null` keeps the per-user windowed sum cheap without bloating writes for the common no-elevation run. The `metadata.elevation_m` key is **left in place** — recap / export / the Strava EF / the Go worker / backup still read it; the column is the additive queryable source and writers populate both going forward.
- **Additive + fail-soft to the recording path.** The column is nullable; a `vert` board sums `coalesce(elevation_gain_m, 0)` so a run with no elevation source contributes 0 (never nulls the sum). The L0/L1 recording stack is untouched.
- **Lockstep extension.** `vert` is added to the `challenges_metric_ck` CHECK *and* the `ChallengeMetric` TS union (a `check_constraint_unions.mjs` PAIR — the guard fails on drift) *and* the `challenge_progress.ts ↔ challenge_progress.dart` parity pair's `metricFromActivity` (reads `summary.elevation_gain_m`). The `activities` view's run branch projects `elevation_gain_m` into its `summary` jsonb so the client-side optimistic estimate reads the SAME number the server sum does. `public_runs` carries the column through (a single ascent integer — public-safe, no geographic signal beyond the rendered polyline).
- **Create is web-canonical.** The metric is offered in the web `ChallengeEditor`; mobile is read-only for challenges (no create form), so the mobile twin only gains the `vert` label + `formatElevationForPref` display in `challengeMetricLabel` / `challengeValueLabel`. Display is unit-aware on both platforms (`formatElevation` → m/ft by the km/mi pref).

## 187. Buyer self-cancel + automated refund couples an `events-cancel` EF (initiate) to the `stripe-events-webhook` (the sole status writer) — the EF never flips `event_orders.status`

**Decided (2026-06-20, migration `20270303_001`).** Paid-events slice P2 (club_events.md) gives a buyer a "Cancel registration" affordance on the event-detail page and automates the refund that P1 left manual. The hard constraint inherited from P1 (§139, the money ledger) is that **`stripe-events-webhook` is the sole, idempotent, service-role-only writer of `event_orders.status`** — so the self-cancel is split across two actors rather than letting the buyer's request mutate the ledger:

- **`events-cancel` Edge Function INITIATES, never transitions.** It authenticates the buyer (JWT), confirms Stripe is configured (else 503, fail-closed exactly like P1 checkout), finds the caller's own cancelable order, and decides via a pure `lib.ts` (`resolveRefundEligibility` + `cancelAction`): a `pending` order expires its Checkout Session at Stripe; a refund-eligible `paid` order creates a Stripe refund (`refund_application_fee: true` — the platform claws back its fee, no profit on a cancelled class) under a stable idempotency key (`events-cancel:<order_id>`) so a double-click can't double-refund; a `paid` order inside the no-refund window 409s `policy_no_refund` and keeps the seat (we never free a seat without returning the money).
- **The webhook still owns the transition.** Stripe's `charge.refunded` is the new third event the webhook handles (`orderStatusTransition` now also maps `paid + charge.refunded -> refunded`): CAS `paid->refunded`, release the seat, reverse the reservation. A replayed `charge.refunded` finds the order already `refunded` and gets `null`, so it can't double-release. `checkout.session.expired` already covered the pending arm.
- **`event_orders.refund_initiated_at` bridges the async gap.** The EF stamps it (a NON-status write) so the UI shows "Refund in progress" between initiate and the webhook's confirmation; on a Stripe failure the EF clears it so the buyer can retry. The migration extends `lock_event_order_status` from a status-lock to a **column-lock**: a non-service-role UPDATE may touch ONLY `refund_initiated_at`, and only on the caller's own `paid` order — every other column (status above all) stays service-role-only. The new buyer UPDATE policy is scoped to `buyer_user_id = auth.uid() AND status = 'paid'`. No new status VALUE (`refunded`/`canceled` already existed), so the `OrderStatus` TS-union ↔ CHECK guard pair is unchanged.
- **Web-only.** P1 checkout is web-only; the cancel UI is too (the buyer's order + refund-eligibility derive in `+page.svelte`, calling `cancelEventOrder` in `data.ts`; the destructive action confirms via `ConfirmDialog` with refundable/no-refund/pending copy). No Dart twin until mobile register lands (P3) — the EF `lib.ts` mirrors the web `paid_registration.ts` refund rule but is not yet a registered parity pair.
- **Prod gate + what's unverified.** With Stripe test keys unset the EF 503s — fail-closed, no real users reached. The **live refund round-trip (real `sk_test_` refund create + signed `charge.refunded` delivery) is UNVERIFIED** without operator keys, recorded as a pre-deploy checklist item per `CLAUDE.md` "compliance gates prod, not code", alongside the same P1 CISO/counsel sign-off on the Stripe Connect marketplace. Pure libs (deno), the column-lock + buyer-policy (pgtap `event_order_self_cancel_test.sql`), and the UI states (Playwright `event-self-cancel.spec.ts`) are all covered.

## 188. USDA FoodData Central is a second food-search source behind one merged shape, queried client-side alongside the keyless Open Food Facts, gated fail-closed on its API key

**Decided (2026-06-20).** Nutrition food search gains USDA FoodData Central (`api.nal.usda.gov`) as a second source next to the keyless Open Food Facts (OFF), behind the existing `food_search` TS↔Dart parity pair (web `food_search.ts` ↔ mobile `food_search.dart`):
- **One result shape, a `source` discriminator (`off` | `usda`).** `parseUsdaSearch` maps the `/v1/foods/search` response into the same `FoodSearchResult` OFF produces — per-100 g macros, keyed on USDA nutrient number `208` for kcal (never `268`/kJ), `203`/`205`/`204` for protein/carbs/fat — so a USDA row flows through the identical tap → confirm-portion → log path. `searchFoodSources` queries both in parallel, **OFF first**, and `dedupeFoods` drops case-insensitive name+brand dupes keeping the first (so a barcoded branded OFF product wins over the looser USDA generic). This coexists with the v1.1 barcode fast-path (`lookupBarcode`), which stays OFF-only.
- **Fail-closed on the key.** OFF needs no key; USDA does. `searchUsda` short-circuits to `[]` on a blank key, and `searchFoodSources` only adds the USDA leg when a key is present — unset → the USDA source is simply absent, OFF still works, no error, no broken composer. The web reads `PUBLIC_USDA_FDC_API_KEY` (client-side via `$env/dynamic/public`); mobile reads `USDA_FDC_API_KEY` from dotenv, guarded by `dotenv.isInitialized` so an unconfigured build (or a test) never throws.
- **Layered resilience across the two sources.** A partial failure degrades to the healthy source — a thrown OFF search with a healthy USDA still returns the USDA rows and vice versa; only when *every* configured source fails does the merge throw, so the composer's retry-vs-empty distinction holds.
- **Why client-side, not a server proxy** (the tradeoff): the FDC key is low-sensitivity (free sign-up, per-key rate-limited) and OFF already runs client-side, so proxying USDA through an Edge Function would add a hop, a cold-start, and a second place to leak the search term for no privacy gain — the search term leaves the client either way. The honest cost is a **new outbound personal-data flow** (`api.nal.usda.gov` receives the typed query): it joins the nutrition pre-real-user-launch privacy checklist as a sub-processor, and the prod gate is the unset key (no key → no flow), so go-live is a deploy-time checklist item, not unwritten code.
- **Not built**: a USDA barcode/`fdcId` direct lookup (only search), persisting the source on the logged `food_log` row beyond web's `<source>:<code>` external_id, source-preference filtering. Deferred.

## 189. Logged-set `set_type` is a real CHECK-constrained column reusing the routine vocabulary, defaulting to `working` — not a metadata key

**Decided (2026-06-20, migration `20270228_001`).** The "RPE / set-type metadata" Gym-mid item is modelled as a first-class column on `gym_sets`, not a `metadata.md` jsonb key:
- **`gym_sets.set_type text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'working'` with a CHECK over the SAME six values as `gym_routine_sets.set_type` (`20270101_001`)**: `warmup` / `working` / `dropset` / `amrap` / `failure` / `backoff`. A routine's planned set role and the logged set it produces now speak one language — the gym session runner already carried `set_type` per step and it flows straight onto the logged row. RPE needed nothing: it has been a column since the Phase 4 foundation (`20261204_001`).
- **A real column, not jsonb**, for the same reason routine sets use one: it is a closed, enforced enum. A CHECK constraint + the `parity-types` `check_constraint_unions.mjs` guard (the `gym_sets.set_type ↔ GymSetType` pair was appended) keep the TS narrow union and the DB in lockstep; jsonb has no such enforcement. The existing `GymSetType` TS union is reused verbatim for both columns.
- **Default `working`, NOT NULL** (the least-surprising choice): a logged set with no explicitly-chosen role is an ordinary working set — the overwhelming common case — so every pre-existing row and every untouched client write stays correct, the column stays dense, and the CHECK stays total. The alternative (nullable, null = "unspecified") would force every reader to special-case null and would make "working" indistinguishable from "not yet typed".
- **Display chips only the non-working roles** (web `setTypeChip` / mobile `_setTypeChip` return null for `working`) so the detail screens stay uncluttered — a wall of "Working" chips on every set is noise; the deviations (warmup, dropset, failure…) are the signal.
- **DSAR rides the existing wildcard**: both export paths (`apps/job_worker` `sel: "*,sets:gym_sets(*)"` + the `export-data` EF `select: '*,sets:gym_sets(*)'`) already select all `gym_sets` columns, so the new column is included with no allowlist to extend.
- **No `gym_prs` / records / set-history RPC change**: set role doesn't enter PR math (a PR is judged on reps × weight / e1RM / volume), so the analytics RPCs were left untouched — adding it there would have been scope creep with no consumer.

## 190. Challenge Browse is a ranked, paginated discovery RPC over a trigger-maintained participant_count, with a creation rate-limit backstop

**Decided (2026-06-23, migration `20270308_001`).** The `/challenges` Browse section used to `select * from challenges order by ends_at` and enrich participant counts client-side — every public challenge, unranked, unbounded. Since open creation is ungated (anyone can make a public challenge — decision recorded in [`challenges.md` Open Question 2](../features/challenges.md)), that surface degrades to noise as volume grows: a throwaway 0-participant board sorts identically to a 5,000-person one.

- **`challenges.participant_count`** is now a trigger-maintained denormalised cache (the [`derived_state.md`](../backend/derived_state.md) contract): recompute-from-`count(*)` on every join/leave via `sync_challenge_participant_count()`, SECURITY DEFINER because a joiner has no UPDATE grant on the challenge row. This is what lets Browse rank + paginate without aggregating `challenge_participants` at read time.
- **`browse_public_challenges(p_search, p_limit, p_offset)`** is the ranked, paginated, searchable discovery feed. Score = `participant_count + joins_7d*2` — total size plus a 7-day join-velocity boost, so a challenge with momentum outranks a stale big one; tie-break ends-soon then newest. Throwaway suppression hides participant-less boards past a 7-day grace window (kept visible to their creator). Pagination is server-side limit/offset (capped at 100) — the web Browse pages it with a Load-more button + a debounced search box.
- **SECURITY DEFINER on the RPC** (unlike the `security invoker` `challenge_leaderboard` / `my_active_challenges`) because the velocity term counts ALL recent joins, which a non-participant caller's RLS on `challenge_participants` would hide and undercount. It is safe: the function only ever returns public challenges and aggregate counts — no per-user rows, no PII — and `auth.uid()` still resolves to the caller under definer.
- **Creation rate-limit, not creation gate**: rather than restrict who can create open challenges (the other horn of Open Question 2), a `before insert on challenges` trigger throttles authenticated creation to 30/hour via the shared `check_rate_limit` bucket. It is guarded to fail OPEN on any rate-limit error and to skip `auth.uid() is null` inserts (seed / service-role / migration), so it can never block a legitimate or test path. Read-layer ranking + write-layer throttle together address "popular over throwaway" without adding a curation/moderation surface.

## 191. Race-day panel checks the goal against current fitness, with symmetric ±2 % / +8 % feasibility bands

**Decided (2026-07-01).** The plan-detail `RaceDayPanel` short-circuited to the runner's goal time whenever one was set, ignoring recent efforts — so a goal typed months earlier was never sanity-checked against fitness that had since moved. The panel now **always** computes the fitness-derived Riegel projection (best qualifying effort, ≥ 1 km, last 90 days) and, when a goal is set, grades it via `goalFeasibility(goalSec, predictedSec)` in the web-only `race_day.ts`.

- **Bands, not a raw delta**: `on_track` is within ±2 % (`GOAL_ONTRACK_BAND`), `far_behind` past +8 % (`GOAL_FARBEHIND_BAND`), with `ahead` / `behind` either side. The 2 % on-track window is deliberately wider than a "you're behind" alarm would need — a single-effort Riegel projection carries several minutes of day-to-day noise on a marathon, so a rounding-scale gap must not cry off-pace. The 8 % far-behind cliff (~+17 min on a 3:30) marks a goal that's materially out of reach vs. one a taper can still close. The band edges are inclusive so the exact boundary reads as the gentler verdict.
- **Suggestion-only, fail-closed**: the signal is hidden entirely with no goal or no qualifying effort — it never fabricates a verdict off no data, and it never rewrites the goal or the plan.
- **Web-only, no twin**: the panel and `race_day.ts` are web-only (there is no mobile race-day surface), so `goalFeasibility` is not a parity pair. If a mobile race-day panel is ever built, port it then.
## 192. The challenge on-pace hint is a pure read-time re-shape of the leaderboard value (`challengePace`), not a new signal or query

**Decided (2026-07-01).** A joined, time-boxed goal challenge showed value vs goal but no sense of whether the runner was on track. Rather than add a projected-completion column, a per-challenge cron, or a second query, the hint is computed entirely client-side from numbers already on hand: the participant's current `value` (the same figure the `challenge_leaderboard` RPC returns) and the challenge window (`starts_at`/`ends_at`, already fetched). `challengePace` (in the `challenge_progress` TS↔Dart pair) derives the even-pace line (`goal × elapsedFraction`), an `ahead`/`on_track`/`behind` verdict within a shared `ON_PACE_BAND` (±5 %), a linear final-value projection, and the daily rate still needed — and returns `upcoming`/`active`/`ended` status so the UI only renders the hint mid-window. Because it re-shapes the SAME value the board computes, an offline-optimistic client estimate can't drift from the server (same property as `metricFromActivity`). The hint self-hides on a goal-less board, before start, after end, and once complete (completion is shown separately). Web renders it in `ChallengeProgressBar` (detail page + dashboard/social panel); mobile mirrors it on `challenge_detail_screen`. No paywall, no compliance gate — engagement polish over the shipped challenges feature.

## 193. App icons are generated from one SVG vector master, not hand-fanned raster files

**Decided (2026-07-01).** The app mark used to exist only as `assets/icon_1024.png` with ~50 platform PNGs (iOS/watchOS icon sets, Android/Wear mipmaps, Garmin launcher, web favicon/touch/192/512) fanned out from it by hand — no vector source, no regeneration path, so every size could drift independently and a design tweak meant editing dozens of blobs. The mark is now a single vector source of truth at `assets/icon.svg` (the existing figure + corner-to-corner orange→magenta gradient, faithfully traced from the old raster so the design is unchanged), and `assets/gen-icons.sh` regenerates every platform target from it.

- **Faithful, not a redesign**: the SVG was `potrace`d from the previous 1024 master's white figure (isolated via the saturation channel) and recomposed over a two-stop linear gradient whose endpoints match the sampled corners — RMSE ≈ 0.05 vs. the original. Changing the look is now a separate, deliberate act of editing the SVG.
- **Deterministic + idempotent regen**: `gen-icons.sh` renders each target directly from the SVG at its committed pixel size (appiconset/mipmap loops are data-driven off the existing files, so sizes can't drift). iOS/watchOS icons are forced to opaque truecolor (`png:color-type=2`, alpha stripped) because Apple rejects any icon carrying alpha — including small icons ImageMagick would otherwise auto-encode as indexed PNGs with a `tRNS` chunk.
- **Depends on the opt-in asset tools** (Inkscape + ImageMagick), consistent with the workstation's asset-pipeline-is-opt-in convention. The script fails closed if either tool is absent.
- **The web header/PWA wordmark is separate**: `apps/web/static/logo-mark.svg` (a rounded-tile mark, used in the sidebar in place of the old raster `icon-192.png`) and `wordmark.svg` (a mark + "Threkir" lockup for share/OG reuse) are hand-authored vector lockups derived from the same mark, plus the previously-missing PWA `manifest.webmanifest` + `theme-color`. These are display chrome, not the packaged app icon, so they live under `apps/web/static/` and are edited directly rather than generated.

## 194. A gym set with both a weight and a duration target is graded on duration while the weight is unrecorded

**Decided (2026-07-02).** `gym_adherence` previously evaluated the weight gate before the duration branch, so a set carrying both a `targetWeightKg` and a `targetDurationS` (e.g. a weighted-vest plank `{targetWeightKg:10, targetDurationS:60}`) logged as `{durationS:75, weightKg:null}` — held 25 % longer, vest load simply not recorded — was graded `missed` on the null weight. Duration is now the **primary axis while the weight isn't recorded**: the weight gate fails a set only when a weight was actually logged and fell short, or when the set has no duration axis at all (the weight-only case, unchanged). A set whose weight *is* logged and falls below the 80 % threshold still misses regardless of duration — recording a short weight is a real miss; omitting the weight on a duration-satisfied hold is not. Applied identically in the `gym_adherence.ts` ↔ `gym_adherence.dart` twin (pinned both sides, 20 tests each). The alternative — treating a missing weight as a hard gate — was rejected as punishing the common, legitimate case where the load axis is incidental and unlogged.

## 195. A first-session gym prescription is labelled `establish_baseline`, not `hold`

**Decided (2026-07-02).** `gym_progression`'s percent-cycle path computes a concrete `suggestedWeightKg` from a 1RM percentage even on the first/bodyweight session, but the `ProgressionReason` was gated on `prescribed > topWeight` and short-circuited to `hold` when `topWeight(lastSets)` was null — so a real starting prescription was mislabelled as "unchanged". A dedicated `establish_baseline` reason now covers the no-prior-top-weight case; the suggested weight is unchanged (the number was always right, only the badge was wrong). `hold` keeps its narrower meaning (a genuine no-change on an established lift), and `increase_weight` its (a raise off a baseline). Added to the `ProgressionReason` union/enum on both platforms and localized in all six web locales + all mobile ARBs, with the one exhaustive switch (`gym_detail_screen`) extended. Twin-pinned in `gym_progression.ts` ↔ `gym_progression.dart` (20 tests each).

## 196. The privacy-zone coarse last-seen carve-out extends to `race_pings`

**Decided (2026-07-02).** Decision-adjacent to the `live_run_pings` carve-out (`20270121_001`): the sibling `race_pings` BEFORE-INSERT trigger (`20260704_001`) still *dropped* every in-zone ping, so a runner who stopped inside their own privacy zone vanished from the event leaderboard's live feed — the same lost-signal ambiguity the solo carve-out fixed. `20270309_001` replaces that trigger so an in-zone race ping is **retained coarsened to a ~2-dp (~1.1 km) grid and flagged `coarse=true`**, keeping at most one such row per `(event_id, instance_start, user_id)` and deleting any prior coarse row so it never accumulates — the race-ping analogue of the `run_id`-keyed solo logic. Only lat/lng are coarsened; `distance_m`/`elapsed_s`/`bpm` are preserved because they back the leaderboard rank and carry no positional precision (`race_pings` has no elevation to strip). The competitive-board-semantics concern that parked this (a coarse dot on a ranked board reads oddly) was resolved in favour of the SAR/last-seen honesty the solo feed already provides — a director tracking a stopped runner needs "last seen near here" more than the board needs positional purity, and the coarse chip makes the approximation explicit. Rendered web-only on the event live page; mobile has no event-live spectator surface reading `race_pings`.

## 197. The mobile centre Log FAB fans a speed-dial above the button; the History Log FAB keeps the bottom sheet

**Decided (2026-07-02).** The centre nav Log FAB (`home_screen.dart`) now opens `showLogSpeedDial` (`widgets/log_speed_dial.dart`) — an `Overlay`-based fan of Run / Lift / Food as **icon-only buttons in a shallow arc around the docked button** (one top-centre, one down-left, one down-right, each riding out along its slot vector) over a dismiss scrim — instead of the `showLogSheet` bottom sheet. A docked `centerDocked` FAB is a natural anchor to fan from, and the options land right under the thumb that pressed the button rather than pulling focus to the bottom edge. The arc + icon-only form (labels on tooltips/semantics, not a vertical stack of labelled rows) keeps the three targets close to the FAB and readable at a glance. The change is **mobile-only** (this nav affordance has no web twin, so it doesn't touch the web-canonical rule), reuses `LogAction` / `orderedLogActions` and the existing i18n keys (no ARB churn), and preserves the `keepRunPrimary` "tap starts a run, long-press opens the picker" semantics. The **History Log FAB** (`runs_screen.dart`) deliberately keeps `showLogSheet`: it isn't a docked FAB with a clean anchor point, and a standard bottom sheet reads correctly there. Two presentations of the same `LogAction` picker is intentional, not drift.

## 198. The app mark is the Old Norse thorn (þ), not a runner glyph

**Decided (2026-07-02).** The original icon was a generic runner-with-speed-lines figure — competent but interchangeable with a dozen rival running apps, and carrying no idea the product name doesn't already carry. The mark is now the **Old Norse letter thorn (þ)**, the initial of *þrek* ("endurance, strength, stamina"), the root from which "Threkir" is built: a lettermark that reads as a confident, slightly foreign "T" to most and as the endurance root to those who know it — fitting for an app whose centre of gravity is ultra / trail / grade-adjusted effort. It trades borrowed iconography for something ownable and true to the name. Three alternates were explored and rejected for the primary slot — the Elder Futhark thorn rune ᚦ (a candidate heritage secondary), an abstract endless-lap ring, and an elevation ridgeline — and are kept as the recorded exploration set (`assets/logo-render/`). The gradient stays the existing ember→magenta but is re-weighted **warm**: ember holds ~58 % of the sweep and magenta only blooms in the far corner, because the palette was never the problem — the generic figure was. The mark is a single flat glyph (balanced ascender/descender, a slightly heavier stem) so it survives down to a 16 px favicon, and it flows through the existing single-master pipeline unchanged (`assets/icon.svg` → `gen-icons.sh` across web / Android / Wear OS / iOS / watchOS / Garmin, §193) plus the hand-authored web `logo-mark.svg` + `wordmark.svg` lockups. Dimensional, ray-traced 3D renders of the mark exist for store / press / splash use but are deliberately **not** the packaged icon — a flat glyph is the correct choice at icon sizes; the procedural Blender render script lives in `assets/logo-render/` (marketing tooling, generated output gitignored).

## 199. Bare / centered loading states use a shared animated-athlete loader (run / train / fuel), not a generic spinner

**Decided (2026-07-02).** Loading affordances that aren't a content-shaped skeleton — a page whose whole body is still fetching, a centered "hold on" moment — now render `ActivityLoader` (`apps/web/src/lib/components/ActivityLoader.svelte`), a small CSS-animated SVG athlete that runs, curls a weight, or eats an apple depending on a `kind` prop (`run` | `train` | `fuel`). It's a single figure rig re-posed per modality so one component covers all three product surfaces, is colored entirely through `var(--color-primary)` (with `color-mix` for shoe/gear/shadow shades) so it inherits the theme, renders `role="status"` + a visually-hidden localized `shell.loading` label for a11y, and honours `prefers-reduced-motion` by freezing every animation. It is **additive, not a replacement for content-matched skeletons** — where a surface already shows a shape-of-the-content placeholder (feed cards, list rows), that stays; the athlete is for the bare/centered cases that would otherwise show nothing or a plain spinner. Wired first into three loading branches that map cleanly to the three kinds — `/runs/heatmap` (run), `/gym/routines` (train), `/nutrition/[date]/[slot]` (fuel). A Flutter twin exists on mobile so the loading personality is consistent across platforms; the web side is unit-covered by type-check plus one Playwright spec (`tests-e2e/gym/activity-loader.spec.ts`) that stalls the routines fetch and asserts the loader's `role="status"` + `<svg>` render in a real loading state.

## 200. checkpoint_crossings health data is scrubbed at 90 days; the split times are permanent results

**Decided (2026-07-03, migration `20270317_001`).** The Art 9 weigh-in / medical fields on `checkpoint_crossings` (`body_weight_kg` / `body_weight_pct` / `medical_hold` / `medical_note`) get a storage-limitation purge, but the purge **scrubs columns rather than deleting rows**: 90 days after `recorded_at` the health fields are nulled (`medical_hold` reset to false) by the `purge-stale-checkpoint-health-data` pg_cron job, while the crossing's in/out stamps survive indefinitely — they ARE the race's split results, with the same permanence expectation as `event_results` (a finisher's splits shouldn't evaporate three months after the race). 90 days mirrors the `notifications` window from `20260922_001`: enough headroom for post-race medical / incident review, defensible under Art 5(1)(e), and the interval lives in one function body. Follows the established purge pattern (SECURITY DEFINER function in `private`, `raise notice` row count, daily off-peak cron slot); pinned by `checkpoint_health_retention_test.sql` and registered in `docs/compliance/retention.md`.

## 201. Views grant SELECT only — Supabase default privileges made auto-updatable views an RLS bypass

**Decided (2026-07-03, migration `20270324_001`).** Supabase's default privileges grant `anon` / `authenticated` FULL table privileges on every object created in the `public` schema. That is safe for tables (RLS gates the rows) but was silently catastrophic for the redacted VIEWS: a simple single-table view is auto-updatable, and a write through a view is authorised against the base table as the VIEW OWNER (`postgres`) — bypassing RLS entirely. Verified live: an anon `POST /rest/v1/public_race_listings` inserted a `race_listings` row past the authenticated-only INSERT policy, and the same shape reached `runs` / `gym_workouts` / `food_log` / `user_profiles` / `race_sessions` / `event_results` through their redacted views (`public_runs` even projects `user_id`, so a forged row could be planted under any user). Every view's defining migration only ever wrote `grant select`; the write bits arrived invisibly from the defaults at CREATE time. `20270324_001` resets all eleven public-schema views to exactly their documented read audiences, and `view_write_privileges_test.sql` pins the invariant with an information_schema catch-all (no public-schema view may grant anon/authenticated anything beyond SELECT) so a future view created without the `revoke all … grant select` reset fails CI instead of shipping writable. House rule: every `create view` migration ends with the revoke-then-grant pair.

## 202. The four embedded-best PR keys are real `runs` columns, not metadata-bag entries

**Decided (2026-07-03, migration `20270325_001`).** `fastest_5k_s` / `fastest_10k_s` / `fastest_half_marathon_s` / `fastest_marathon_s` moved out of the `runs.metadata` jsonb bag into typed nullable integer columns — the same "should be a column" bar `activity_type` / `is_dnf` cleared in `20261207_001` (db-design audit High). The keys were never free-form annotations: `refresh_personal_records_for_user` filtered, cast, and regex-validated them in SQL on every refresh, so each of the four embedded-best UNION branches paid a per-row `metadata ? key` probe + `->>` cast + `~ '^[0-9]+$'` guard that a typed column replaces with a plain `is not null` read (`>= 0` preserves the old regex's non-negative domain). The promotion also narrows the statement trigger's UPDATE watch list from the whole `metadata` value to the four columns, so a title/notes/bag-only edit no longer forces a full PR recompute for the user, and it gives both row-type generators visibility the bag never had. Production-safety shape: catalog-only nullable column adds; a backfill that touches only bag-carrying rows (`?|`), rewrites each exactly once (columns set + keys stripped in the same UPDATE) in bounded 5000-row batches, and runs with the PR update trigger disabled — the rewritten refresher produces identical `personal_records` rows from the moved values, so mid-migration recomputes would be pure waste. Clients keep the keys in the in-memory Dart `Run.metadata` carrier (single read path) while the api_client save path lifts them onto the columns — the established `activity_type` carrier pattern — and `public_runs` exposes them as columns since they were already public-safe bag keys.

## 203. GoTrue auth emails go through the send-email hook to a localized Edge Function, not per-locale template files

**Decided (2026-07-04).** The i18n-readiness audit's one High server finding: signup confirmation, password recovery, magic link, and email-change mail were GoTrue's built-in English-only templates, while every product email (§120) was already localized. GoTrue's `template_path` mechanism can't fix this — GoTrue doesn't know the recipient's locale at send time, and per-locale template files would fork the copy away from the existing catalogue. Instead the **send-email auth hook** points at a new `auth-email` Edge Function that mirrors the worker pipeline: Standard Webhooks signature verification as the sole trust boundary (`verify_jwt = false` — GoTrue sends no JWT; missing secret/signature fails closed 503/401), locale resolved `user_settings.prefs.locale` → signup-time `user_metadata.locale` → `en`, the same six-locale catalogue shape and visual layout as `email_i18n.go`, GoTrue-byte-compatible verify links (including the secure-email-change reversed `token_hash`/`token_hash_new` pairing and the unencoded-`redirect_to` quirk, so the web e2e reset flow is untouched), and the worker's `SMTP_*` env contract over a minimal Deno SMTP client. Unknown action types render an informational default rather than failing — a hook failure would surface as a broken signup/reset, so the function only 5xxs when the mail genuinely cannot be sent. Locally the hook secret is a committed dev value in `config.toml` mirrored in the committed `supabase/functions/.env` (auto-loaded on `supabase start`), because an `env()` reference would hand GoTrue an empty signing key on any machine without the var and take down `supabase start` / all auth mail; prod enables the hook in the hosted dashboard with a real secret (pre-launch ops item — until then prod falls back to the built-in templates). The duplicated catalogue (Deno vs Go) is deliberate: the alternative — enqueueing auth mail through the jobs queue — would put a queue hop inside time-sensitive auth flows and still need a hook. `docs/features/email.md § GoTrue auth emails`.

## 204. Server-side route generation is the second Pro perk; the free tier keeps the in-browser heuristic

**Decided (2026-07-05).** The engine-backed "Generate a route by distance" path (`/api/routes/generate` → graph_cycle + GraphHopper) now requires a user JWT and a true `is_pro()`, mirroring the route-describe gate; free and anonymous callers fall back to the in-browser OSRM heuristic, which stays the free tier. Rationale: this is the one deferred-service feature whose Pro-gating is honest product tiering rather than cost theater — the Fly engines are fixed-cost, but the perk is real (real street-graph loops vs a radial heuristic), power-user-shaped, and mirrors the proven free-baseline / Pro-enhancement pattern the AI route description already uses. Two honest-selling rules are load-bearing: (1) the handler answers **501 (engines unconfigured) BEFORE the tier gate**, so a deploy with the engines deferred (rock-bottom AND Lean both defer them) tells the client "not available", never "upgrade" — no upsell for a perk the deploy can't deliver; (2) the perk gets its own fail-closed client flag, `PUBLIC_ROUTE_GEN_ENABLED` (`route_gen_flag.ts`, mirroring `coach_flag.ts`), gating the `/routes/new` upsell and the Pro-card bullet, and the storefront's sellable condition widens from `coachOn` to `coachOn || routeGenOn` — this replaces the old "every Pro perk is a Coach feature" invariant in paywall.md. The viewer JWT rides `x-supabase-authorization` (CloudFront's OAC owns `Authorization`), which required whitelisting that header on the generate-route origin request policy and handing the Lambda the Supabase URL + anon key; a missing env or a failed `is_pro()` RPC denies the perk (fail-closed 500) rather than granting it. `bypassPaywallEnabled` is wrapper-computed dev-only, exactly like coach/route-describe. Free callers cost the engines nothing (the 403 is returned before any engine fetch). `security_guards.test.ts` pins the storefront condition, the per-perk bullet gates, and the header slot; the generate unit tests pin the 501-before-403 order and every gate verdict.

## 205. Public entity SEO uses dedicated `/share/*` SSR pages behind ONE shared entity-SSR Lambda; www 301s to the apex

**Decided (2026-07-06).** The web app is a statically-prerendered SPA (`adapter-static`), so any route rendered client-side (dashboard, `/u/[id]`, `/clubs/[slug]`, `/races`, most of the app) hands a crawler — and every social link-unfurler, which run no JS — the empty `index.html` shell. The existing per-entity share Lambdas (`share-run/route/badge/recap`) already solved this for those surfaces by injecting per-entity `<head>` at request time. This decision extends that pattern to four more public surfaces — **club events, public profiles, public clubs, and race-calendar listings** — via dedicated public routes `/share/{event/[id],profile/[id],club/[slug],race/[id]}` (anon, shell-less, `prerender = false`), each with a pure `share_<x>_lookup` (anon-readable rows only — public-club RLS for events, `public_profile_by_id` RPC for profiles, `is_public` clubs, anon `race_listings`) + a pure `share_<x>_meta` builder emitting canonical + Open Graph + Twitter + schema.org JSON-LD (`SportsEvent`/`Event` for events + races, `ProfilePage`+`Person` for profiles, `SportsOrganization` for clubs). Two deliberate departures from the run/route/badge/recap precedent: **(1) one shared `share-entity` Lambda** dispatches all four paths (identical shape: lookup → `build*Head` → `render*HeadTags` → generic `injectEntityHead`; none needs a per-entity og:image PNG, so the OG image is the branded card or an avatar URL and the Lambda is HTML-only at 256 MB) instead of cloning the ~10-resource share-badge stack four times — the alarms carry four per-surface `upstream_unreachable` metric filters over the one log group because the lookups log `[share-event]`/`[share-profile]`/etc.; **(2) profiles are NOT enumerated in `sitemap.xml`** (events/clubs/races are) — a full people-directory crawl manifest is not wanted, so profiles stay link-discoverable only. Privacy is preserved end-to-end: event JSON-LD carries only the club's coarse `location_label`, never the precise meet coordinate (§147); race JSON-LD omits the `location_point` geometry; profile JSON-LD carries only the anon-safe display name + avatar. Marketing/brand SEO is closed out alongside: a reusable `SeoHead.svelte` + `Organization`/`WebSite` JSON-LD on the landing page, `share/run` gains the canonical + `WebPage` JSON-LD its `share/route` sibling already had, a `color-scheme` meta + Supabase `preconnect` improve first paint under the hash-based CSP (which forbids a hand-written inline theme-bootstrap script), and a viewer-request CloudFront Function 301s any `www.*` host to the bare apex (both aliased the same distribution and split ranking signal). The four SvelteKit routes run the same lookup + meta under `vite dev`, so every surface is verifiable locally; the shared Lambda + its CloudFront/OIDC/release wiring is landed but **deploy-gated** (needs `terraform apply` + a first `web@*` deploy), the same posture as the other share Lambdas. `docs/features/seo.md`.

## 206. shadow_hidden is enforced at the base-table RLS layer for clubs + events, not just per-query

**Decided (2026-07-06).** `shadow_hidden` (the report-driven auto-hide, §172 / migration 20270218_001) is contractually supposed to drop a target from *every* public/search/discovery surface, but enforcement was per-consumer: the `public_routes`/`public_profiles` views, the `is_public_{club,event,route}_by_id` definers, and `search_clubs`/`search_user_profiles` each filtered `and not shadow_hidden` by hand, while the **base-table SELECT RLS policies for `clubs` + `events` gated only on `is_public`**. So any *direct* anon/authenticated read of those tables saw hidden rows unless the caller remembered the filter — and one did forget: the `/share/{club,event}` SEO surfaces + the sitemap surfaced moderator-hidden clubs/events until the per-query filters were added. Migration 20270328_001 makes the exclusion the RLS-enforced **default**: the "public clubs are readable by anyone" policy gains `and shadow_hidden = false`, and the events policy's club-public branch gains `and clubs.shadow_hidden = false` (events have no such column, so the parent club's flag governs). To keep the moderation contract's "owner + admins still see their own row" guarantee, the club member/owner SELECT policy was broadened from `is_public = false and (owner or member)` to just `(owner or member)`, so an owner/active member keeps visibility of their own club even when it's public-and-shadow-hidden. Admin moderation review is untouched — it runs through SECURITY DEFINER RPCs (`fetch_reports_for_target` / `admin_unhide_target`) that bypass RLS, and there is no admin-read RLS policy anywhere, so none was added (a hidden public club is now, correctly, invisible to a non-member admin's *live* page read, exactly as a hidden private club already was — review happens via the moderation queue, not the live page). This is a backstop **under** the per-query `shadow_hidden` filters the SEO surfaces already carry, not a replacement — layered defense for a moderation/privacy property. Verified with role-scoped RLS checks (anon + non-member → excluded; owner + member → still visible) and pinned by `rls_shadow_hidden_backstop_test.sql`. RLS-only change, so no generated-type drift. `docs/features/seo.md` + `docs/backend/api_database.md § Auto-hide on reports`.

**Amended (2026-07-06, migration 20270329_001).** The same-day audit/rls + audit/storage passes found the backstop incomplete on two sibling surfaces, both now fixed. (1) **`user_profiles`**: the base SELECT policy (20260521_001, any-authenticated with no `shadow_hidden` clause) and the `public_profiles` view (20260824_001, no `WHERE` at all) still resolved a hidden user's display name + avatar — and the ~25 unfiltered `.from('user_profiles')` enrichment call sites in the web data layer kept rendering them in kudos/comment/follower lists. The policy is now `auth.uid() = id or shadow_hidden = false` (owner carve-out mirrors the clubs one) and the view carries `where shadow_hidden = false`. (2) **routes**: `private.is_route_visible_to` + `clip_route_for_viewer` gained the clause on their public branch. The non-obvious trap worth remembering: those two are SECURITY DEFINER functions that read `routes` directly, so they do **not** inherit the base table's RLS the way the club-photos storage policy's plain `join clubs` inherits the clubs backstop by composability — a DEFINER visibility helper needs the moderation clause spelled out in its own body, and any future one must copy it. Because five tables (`route_photos`/`route_reviews`/`segments`/`route_markers`/`route_conditions`) and the `route-photos` bucket all gate on the helper, the single-clause fix closed the whole direct-by-id family at once. Owner + active-club-member visibility of hidden routes is retained (same soft-hide contract). Pinned by `rls_shadow_hidden_backstop_pt2_test.sql` (10 assertions) + the `storage_bucket_privacy_test.sql` policy-content pins.

---

## 207. The safety net escalates on telemetry silence via a pg_cron scan — one alert per run, email-only, everything fail-closed

**Decided (2026-07-06).** The persona-woman CRITICAL finding: both "safety" surfaces failed her — `safety_contacts` only fired *after* a successful save (the one scenario it exists for, a run that ends badly mid-way, never INSERTs), and the Settings→Account trusted-contacts card persisted a prefs list nothing read, under copy promising overdue alerts. The safety net (docs/features/safety.md) is: auto-live-share on run start behind a device pref, plus `enqueue_safety_overdue_emails()` — a 5-minute pg_cron scan that emails every confirmed safety contact ONCE when a live-broadcast run has gone silent past the runner's `safety_overdue_minutes` pref (migration `20270401_001`).

**The shape, and why.**
- **Trigger = telemetry silence, not an expected-return input.** A per-run "back by X" prompt adds start friction the persona explicitly doesn't want (the point is that it's automatic); silence (latest `live_run_pings.at`, floored at `started_at` when no ping ever landed) also catches phone-destroyed/crashed cases an ETA can't. The known false positive — a long no-signal stretch — is handled by copy ("this can also mean loss of phone signal"), the runner-chosen window, and the once-per-run cap, not by pretending it can't happen.
- **Once per run, stamped in-statement.** The scan's CTE stamps `runs.metadata.safety_escalated_at` in the same statement that selects the matches, so a concurrent double-fire of the cron can't enqueue twice. No re-arm if pings resume and stop again (v1); the existing finish email is the all-clear.
- **Channel = the existing `safety_email` kind, third template `overdue`** — same §131 rationale (not gated on the runner's `email_notifications`; recipient may be a non-user). Payload carries times + the `/live/{run_id}` link, NEVER coordinates — the live page already applies privacy-zone clipping on both transports. SMS was deliberately dropped along with the trusted-contacts scaffold's phone fields: no SMS provider exists in the stack, and storing phone numbers under a "Safety" heading was liability without capability.
- **Fail-closed everywhere.** No pref (or <10 min, or malformed) → no match; no confirmed contact → no match; scan bounded to runs started in the last 24 h so ancient crashed stubs can't fire when a user sets the pref later; `auto_live_share` defaults off because the broadcast stub is public-by-link. CISO/counsel review of the email copy is a pre-deploy checklist item, not a code gate (§150).
- **The stub-transition fix rode along because auto-share would have amplified it:** `beginLiveBroadcast` INSERTs the stub row, so `enqueue_safety_finish_emails` + `notify_run_completed` fired a bogus "finished" at share START and nothing at the real save (an upsert-UPDATE). Both now skip `metadata.in_progress` rows and fire on the stub→saved transition.

**Trade-offs.** The scan reads a prefs-bag key in SQL (a documented exception to the bag-keys-are-never-queried placement rule, same class as the worker-read email prefs) — a column would be over-modelling for one predicate. Escalation only covers live-broadcast runs: no pings, no signal — the settings copy says so, and auto-live-share existing is what makes that a reasonable contract. **Don't** add re-arming or a second alert without revisiting alert fatigue; **don't** put coordinates in the email; **don't** resurrect a phone/SMS field without an actual SMS channel.

## 208. Firmware simulation runs the unmodified ELF on Renode with a custom defmt-RTT drain

**Decided (2026-07-08).** `bin/watch-sim.sh` boots the exact `thumbv7em-none-eabihf` release ELF that `watch-flash.sh` flashes — nothing compiled differently — on Renode's emulated nRF52840 DK, so firmware work proceeds with no dev kit attached (tier-1 parts still unordered per §71). Renode over QEMU because QEMU's only Nordic machine is the nRF51; Renode 1.16 models the Cortex-M4 plus the RTC/CLOCK/UARTE/GPIO set Embassy's runtime needs. Two non-obvious pieces: (1) defmt logs travel over RTT, but Renode's bundled `segger-rtt.py` hooks the SEGGER C library's function symbols, which the pure-Rust `defmt-rtt` crate doesn't export — so `apps/custom_watch/sim/defmt_rtt.py` polls the `_SEGGER_RTT` control block in emulated RAM on a managed thread, drains new bytes to a file that host-side `defmt-print -e <elf>` decodes live, and advances the read offset; the alternative (a `defmt-serial` build variant) was rejected because it would fork the firmware between sim and hardware. (2) A canned NMEA fixture (`sim/nmea/bench_jog.nmea`) feeds the emulated UARTE0 at the 1 Hz fix rate a real MAX-M10S emits, so the step-3 GNSS bring-up can be developed entirely in the sim. Deliberately not simulatable: BLE (nrf-softdevice needs the real radio), power draw, and sensor analog behaviour — those stay on-target. Workflow + gotchas in `apps/custom_watch/local_testing.md` § Simulating without a board.

## 209. The watch sim runs the whole tier-1 experience: emulated display, sim phone link, dev-gated mobile screen

**Decided (2026-07-08), extending §208.** Three linked choices. **(a) The firmware grew a hardware-free core crate.** `watch_core` (fix model + RMC/GGA accumulator, face layout, phone-link frame encoding) sits between the parsers/drivers and the Embassy tasks, so `app/` tasks are thin peripheral glue, the pure logic is host-tested, and the same core ports unchanged to tier-2 silicon — this is what keeps the 60-70% host-testable split real rather than aspirational. **(b) The display is simulated at the wire protocol, not stubbed.** `sim/SharpMipDisplay.cs` is a runtime-compiled Renode peripheral decoding the exact Sharp MIP line-update packets `drivers/sharp_mip` emits (CS-GPIO framing, LSB-first byte layout, white-is-1 polarity), rendered via Renode's `AutoRepaintingVideo` (`watch-sim.sh --gui` opens the window). The display rides SPIM3 (0x4002F000): Renode's stock platform declares spi0..2 without EasyDMA, repl overlays cannot change a constructor argument, and the SPIM3 slot is free to declare fresh with `easyDMA: true` — it is also the better production instance (dedicated high-speed SPIM). **(c) BLE is bridged, not faked.** The step-6 GATT characteristic's payload — `watch_core::link` NDJSON status frames — streams today from a `phone` firmware task over UARTE1, which the sim bridges to TCP (default 7788). The mobile app's Settings → Developer → "Sim watch link" screen (visible only against a loopback backend, the same isolation rail as the seed auto-login) connects and renders the live values, so watch↔phone integration is demonstrable on a workstation with zero hardware; step 6 swaps the pipe for nrf-softdevice and the phone side swaps the socket for a BLE client, with the frame decoder (`sim_watch_link.dart`) unchanged. **Don't** grow the sim screen into a product surface — pairing/sync UX pioneers on web per §24 when the watch becomes a product track; this screen is dev tooling behind the loopback gate.

## 210. Tier-1 BLE (S140 SoftDevice) is a compile-verified, feature-gated build — mutually exclusive with the sim, off by default
