**Decided (2026-07-08), implementing README step 6.** The watch's phone radio uses Nordic's **S140 SoftDevice** via the git-only `nrf-softdevice` crate (pinned rev `47d6121c`), which resolves cleanly against this Embassy 0.10 stack because its core depends only on `embassy-sync 0.8` + `embassy-futures 0.1` + `heapless 0.9` — all already this workspace's pins. Four linked choices. **(a) It is a Cargo feature, not the default.** `ble` is off by default; the whole SoftDevice path compiles only under `cargo build --no-default-features --features ble`. This is forced by `critical-section`: the SoftDevice must provide the impl, cortex-m provides it in every other build, and two impls is a link error — and Cargo features are purely additive, so a `ble` feature can't *remove* `cortex-m/critical-section-single-core`. The provider therefore lives in a default `single-core-cs` feature that the BLE build opts out of via `--no-default-features`. **(b) `build.rs` swaps the linker script by feature.** `CARGO_FEATURE_BLE` selects `memory-ble.x` (app linked above the S140 at flash 0x27000, RAM +31 KiB) over the flat `memory.x`; the default/sim memory map is byte-for-byte unchanged, so nothing that works today regresses. **(c) The payload is the existing link frame, unchanged.** The `ble` task advertises a custom 128-bit GATT service and `notify`s one `watch_core::link` status frame per second — the same bytes the UART/sim transport emits (§209), so the phone-side decoder stays transport-agnostic. On the BLE build `main` spawns the `ble` task in place of the UARTE1 `phone` task; peripheral interrupt priorities move to P2 to clear the SoftDevice's reserved 0/1/4. **(d) It ships unverified, and that is acknowledged, not hidden.** The Renode sim cannot run the proprietary SoftDevice, and there is no dev kit yet, so this path is *compile-and-link-verified only* — RAM origin (the SoftDevice reports the true value at boot), interrupt priorities, and connection params all need bench confirmation, enumerated in `local_testing.md` § Building + flashing the BLE firmware. Writing it now (behind a fail-safe-off gate that CI never builds and the sim never loads) is deliberate: it proves the dependency stack is viable — the one real §80 "blocking driver issue" risk for Rust+Embassy — and leaves only on-hardware validation for when parts arrive. **Out of scope, still open:** chunked *finished-run* file sync (`run_manifest`/`run_chunk`), which is blocked on an on-device LittleFS storage layer tier 1 doesn't have; ANT+ (needs the S340 SoftDevice); and secure pairing/bonding.

## 211. Owner override: build the full watch→phone→Supabase run-sync vertical now, despite the tier-2 gate

**Decided (2026-07-08) by the owner, overriding the §71 tier-2 gate + the `apps/custom_watch/CLAUDE.md` "watch-to-Supabase sync is tier-2+ … shouldn't exist yet" deferral.** The remaining custom-watch coding after step 6 (BLE live link) was the finished-run *sync* — get a recorded run off the watch, into the phone, into Supabase. The scope docs classified that as gated tier-2 integration work; the owner elected to build the whole vertical now anyway (it's an owner-personal research effort, and §92's "build the best watch ever, scope/effort not constraints" governs the long-term). This ADR records that the gate was consciously overridden, not overlooked — a future reader should not treat the presence of sync code as evidence the §71 triggers flipped. **Design choices that fell out:** (a) **The watch stores the track**, which the recorder didn't — the `record` task streams every accepted fix to flash during recording (an ultra's 360k points can't live in RAM), and the flash log *is* the syncable run. (b) **A compact binary wire format** (`watch_core::run_store`, host-tested with a golden vector) — `header(16) | point[N](16) | footer(20)`, lat/lon in 1e-7°, altitude in decimetres, a CRC32 the phone verifies — rather than gzipped-JSON-on-the-MCU (wasteful on a Cortex-M; the phone has the resources to reformat). This **revises firmware.md's "phone is a pass-through"**: the phone now *reformats* the wire blob into the JSON payload the existing `WatchIngestQueue` / `runFromWatchPayload` already ingests, so the watch reuses the shipped phone→Supabase upload path with zero backend/schema change. (c) **Reuse the existing ingest path**: the mobile side already had `WatchIngestQueue.drain` → `runFromWatchPayload` → `api.saveRun` (built for the Apple/Wear companions); the custom watch's BLE client feeds the same queue, so "the Supabase leg" needed no new code. (d) **BLE framing** — a `run_manifest` characteristic (finished runs: seq + size + start-uptime, plus a uptime anchor for `started_at`) and a `run_chunk` request/notify characteristic (phone writes `{run_seq, offset, len}`, watch notifies the slice); both wire formats live in `run_store` so the two ends can't drift. Everything BLE-side stays behind the `ble` feature and is **compile-verified only** (no SoftDevice in the sim, no dev kit) — the `run_store` core + the Dart decoder are the parts that are genuinely host-tested, pinned to a shared golden vector on both sides. `started_at` is derived from the uptime anchor (sync happens after the run), an acknowledged approximation until the watch has a real RTC or parses GPS date.

## 212. Watch run-sync: phone BLE stack is flutter_reactive_ble, and the blob CRC covers header+points (footer totals recomputable)

**Decided (2026-07-08), implementing the phone half of §211.** Two specifics that fell out of building the mobile side. **(a) BLE package = `flutter_reactive_ble`, not `flutter_blue_plus`.** The estate already ships `flutter_reactive_ble` (BSD-3) for the chest-strap HR path and deliberately rejected `flutter_blue_plus` (its 2.x per-call license gate). The watch-sync client wraps the same sanctioned stack behind the injectable `WatchBleTransport` seam — no second BLE dependency, no pubspec change. Since the real transport can't run without a paired watch + a SoftDevice radio anyway, the seam (not the radio library) is what carries the tested behaviour: the whole pull→decode→verify→reshape→deliver path is unit-tested against a fake transport replaying the golden blob. **(b) `run_store`'s CRC32 covers header + points only, not the footer.** The footer's `distance_m`/`moving_s`/`elapsed_s` totals sit outside the CRC window, so a bit-flip there passes `verify_blob`. That's acceptable and deliberate: the track points (the irreplaceable data) are CRC-protected, and the totals are recomputable from the points on the phone — they're a convenience/cross-check, not the source of truth. `verify_blob` catches the failure modes that matter (a corrupt or truncated track, a cut-off transfer). Both the Rust `run_store` test and the Dart `sim_watch_sync` test pin the identical golden hex vector, so the two decoders cannot silently diverge.

## 213. Sim button injection uses a poll-in-the-sim feature, not a firmware-wide change — Renode doesn't model the nRF GPIO SENSE path

**Decided (2026-07-08).** The Renode sim couldn't drive BTN1/BTN2/BTN3, so page navigation (§ step 4) and manual start/stop were bench-only. Root cause: embassy-nrf's plain `Input::wait_for_falling_edge` rides the nRF52840 GPIO **SENSE/DETECT + PORT-event** mechanism (the low-power path — it wakes from System OFF and shares one PORT interrupt across all sense pins), and Renode's nRF52840 GPIO model implements only the pin-level IN register, not SENSE/DETECT/LATCH. So the edge future never wakes in the sim, no matter how the pin is driven.

Two options were rejected: (a) implement SENSE/DETECT/LATCH + the PORT event in a custom Renode C# GPIO model — heavy, brittle, reimplements nRF internals; (b) switch the production button task to GPIOTE `InputChannel` (IN events, which Renode *does* model) — but on the nRF52 those keep a clock running and cost meaningfully more idle current than SENSE, a real regression on a watch whose whole firmware story is power discipline. So the chosen path mirrors the existing `sim-autostart`: a **default-OFF `sim-buttons` Cargo feature** that swaps *only the sim build's* button task for a level-polling variant (Renode reliably models the IN register), driven from `sim/watch.resc` monitor macros that pull the input pin low via `gpio0 OnGPIO` (a `Miscellaneous.Button`'s `Press` doesn't propagate on these pins; a bare `btn3` doesn't run a Renode macro — it's `runMacro $btn3`). The hardware build keeps the SENSE path byte-for-byte. This is the same "sim-only accommodation, never degrade the hardware firmware" principle as § 208's autostart.

Wiring the buttons immediately surfaced a real screen-task bug (fixed same day): the render loop applied state via `try_changed()` at the top but then `select`ed on the receivers' `changed()` futures and discarded their return values. Because `changed()` advances the receiver's seen-marker when it resolves, a **one-shot** change consumed by the select — a `state::PAGE` switch, a `state::INTERACTION` stamp — was lost before `try_changed()` could observe it. Continuously-updated signals (`FIX`/`RECORD`) self-healed on the next tick and hid the bug; `PAGE` only changes on a button press, so the page never switched on screen even though the state cycled. Fix: apply the value the winning `changed()` branch yields. Sim-verified by dumping the panel before/after a page switch.

## 214. Tier-1 on-run alerts anchor their fuel cadence to fuel_plan's defaults, bank on moving time, and share one display slot

**Decided (2026-07-10).** The watch's first on-run alerts (the roadmap backlog's "drink / eat / nutrition reminders — the ultra-critical one" plus an HR-zone ceiling) needed cadence numbers, and per § 24 the firmware doesn't get to invent them. `watch_core::alerts` reduces the main app's race-fueling defaults (web `routes/fuel_plan.ts`, canonical, Dart twin) to fixed moving-time intervals: 60 g carbs/hr at 25 g per gel → **eat every 1500 s**, 500 ml fluid/hr at a ~125 ml soft-flask sip → **drink every 900 s**. The intervals bank against `Snapshot::moving_s` — the same bank the HR-zone accumulators use — so a manual pause, auto-pause, or aid-station stop accrues nothing and the reminder means "you've been *running* 15 minutes since the last sip", not "time passed". Alerts share **one display slot** with an 8 s TTL: the HR-zone alert (off by default; a plausibility-guarded 1..=4 ceiling setter like `set_max_hr`, awaiting a settings sync) outranks and supersedes a fuel reminder because over-effort is actionable now, but the superseded reminder **re-queues rather than drops** — fuel is the one alert an ultra runner must never silently lose. The zone alert fires once per excursion (re-arms only when the zone drops back to the ceiling, plus a 60 s cooldown against boundary flapping). Display-only by design — the DK has no vibration motor — rendered as a `!`-prefixed all-caps 2x banner over the hero band (a 1-bit reflective panel has no inverse-video or colour to spend); the engine is pure, driven by the record task *after* the recorder updates, so an alert can never disturb the recording math (L4). The sim shortens the cadences through the same public setter (gated under `sim-autostart`), never the hardware defaults. Pace / distance / time alerts stay T2 backlog.

## 215. Tier-1 cut-off ETA feeds the course-agnostic recorder a route position; the race predictor projects the current run as a single effort

**Decided (2026-07-10).** Two parity ports landed together (`watch_core::cutoff_eta` from web `runs/live_cutoff_eta.ts`, `watch_core::race_predictor` from `training/race_predictor.ts`), and each raised the same question: what watch-appropriate *input* feeds an algorithm the app runs against data the standalone tier-1 watch doesn't have. Both answers keep the ported logic byte-for-byte and change only the input, so neither pioneers a product feature (§ 24).

**Cut-off ETA** needs a distance-along-route the recorder doesn't compute — that lives in the `nav` task's course projection (§ course work). Rather than teach the recorder about courses, the `nav` task feeds it the scalar `along_m` through `Recorder::set_route_position`, the same external-input shape as `set_hr` / `set_baro_altitude` / `set_pacer_goal`; the recorder stays course-agnostic and folds the cut-off ETA into its snapshot only when cutoff legs are loaded (empty on hardware until a BLE course-push lands). The port's honesty gate is preserved and given teeth on-device: the fed position is **stamped with the clock**, and once it ages past a stale budget (scaled to the GNSS mode's fix interval) — or the pace is unknown — the ETA is withheld (`Unknown`) instead of projecting off a frozen fix. A one-fix scheduling lag between the `nav` and `record` tasks is accepted (the ETA is a slowly-varying projection; the record task reads `NAV` via `try_changed()` like it already does for the GNSS mode).

**Race predictor** takes a *pool* of past efforts; a standalone tier-1 watch has no history store to query, so the recorder projects the **current run treated as a single effort** (age 0). This is the only effort source available, and it makes the page a live "at this fitness, your race times are…" — useful mid-run and clearly the same laddered prediction the web dashboard shows. Because a Riegel projection off a 200 m warm-up is noise, the recorder gates the surface on a 1 km `MIN_PREDICT_DISTANCE_M` input-validity threshold — one distance tier up from the `avg_pace` "not meaningful yet" guard, and not a change to the ported algorithm. When the future BLE history sync lands (tier 2) the same `predict_race_ladder` can be fed the full effort pool with no code change.

Both results ride `Snapshot` (Shape A), so their glance pages render uniformly from `snap` with zero ui-task change, and both keep honest inactive states (`NO CUTOFFS` / `NO CUTOFF AHEAD` / `--`; `NEED 1 KM`) rather than zeros pretending to be data.

## 216. Tier-1 watch UI splits pixel-widget drawing into a host-testable `watch_render` crate

The glance pages were text-only (`watch_core::face` → rows of characters), which the ui task blitted through the 8x16 font. To make the mid-run numbers *glanceable as shapes* — a pacer ahead/behind bar, gear/fuel gauges, per-zone bars, a pace-distribution histogram, a GPS signal meter, a page-position indicator, and triple-size hero numbers — the UI needed real pixel drawing, not more glyphs.

The load-bearing decision is **where that drawing lives**. Drawing needs both the `sharp_mip` framebuffer and `watch_core` state, and it had previously been done inline in the `app`'s `ui` task — which only builds for the board, so none of it was reachable by `cargo test` (the same gap that leaves the nav-panel + trackback overlays untested). Rather than grow that untested surface, the pixel geometry is factored three ways:

- **Primitives** live in the `sharp_mip` driver (`fill_rect` / `stroke_rect` / `hline` / `vline` / `draw_progress_bar` / `draw_center_bar` / `draw_text_3x`), host-tested in the driver crate, clipping-not-panicking like the existing `draw_line` / `draw_text_2x`.
- **Pure geometry** — *what* fraction / how many bars / which page-dot — lives in new `watch_core` modules (`statusbar`, `gauge`, `bar_chart`), `no_std` and unit-tested alongside the rest of core, with the honest-inactive discipline the pages already use (a zero-time zone draws no bar; a sensorless run frames nothing; a division guards against `NaN`).
- **Composition** — turning that geometry into pixels over a framebuffer — is a new **`watch_render` crate** (`no_std`, depends on `sharp_mip` + `watch_core`) whose `draw_*_overlay(fb, snap, …)` functions fold in the `gauge`/`statusbar` math and are exercised by pixel-asserting unit tests **plus an ASCII-preview harness** (`render/src/preview.rs`) that dumps a composed page as text — the stand-in for a display sim on a host without Renode.

The `ui` task shrinks to the peripheral glue it should be: it calls `widgets::draw_*` into the cells `face` leaves blank on the matching page, after the text rows. Widgets dirty nothing when their inputs are unchanged (the framebuffer's per-line compare), so a resting page still flushes zero SPI — the same power contract the rest of the face keeps.

Two `face` layout changes pair with this: the zone / split rows drop their `#`-glyph bars (the pixel bars own those cells now), and the single-metric Distance / Pace pages reserve rows 0–2 for a `draw_text_3x` hero with the label moved to row 3. `f32::clamp` is used for the fraction clamps — it's a comparison-based method in `core`, so it links in these `no_std` crates (only the transcendental float ops need `libm`); the earlier hand-rolled comparison clamps were removed once the clippy `manual_clamp` gate flagged them.

This advances the § 90 / `local_testing.md` "keep 60–70 % host-testable" goal by moving drawing — previously in the untestable 30–40 % — into a crate `cargo test` reaches, and it ports forward: the `watch_render` split is the same shape a tier-2 colour panel would reuse (geometry unchanged, only the primitive backend swapped).

## 217. Tier-1 watch settings sync: a binary phone→watch frame decoded into the existing setter hooks

Several recorder features shipped with a plausibility-guarded settings-sync *setter* but nothing driving it — `Recorder::set_max_hr` / `set_pacer_goal` / `set_gear` and `AlertEngine::set_zone_ceiling` were armed only by hardcoded sim demos (a 1 km/5:00 pacer, a 700/800 km shoe). This wires the real phone→watch config push into those hooks.

Decisions:

- **Binary wire, not JSON.** The watch is `no_std` with no allocator and no JSON parser, and the run-sync side already speaks fixed-layout little-endian frames (`run_store`). `watch_core::settings` matches that: a 4-byte magic (`SET1`), a version byte, a presence-bitfield, then only the present fields in bit order (max HR `u16`, pacer `u32`+`u32`, gear `f32`+`f32`, zone ceiling `u8`). Every field is optional so a partial push (just a new max HR) leaves the rest untouched. A JSON frame would have meant pulling a parser into the firmware for one small message.
- **Decode parses, setters guard.** `WatchSettings::decode` only turns bytes into a struct; the plausibility rules stay in the setters it feeds (`set_max_hr`'s 100–240 bpm window, `Pacer::set_goal`'s bounds, `set_zone_ceiling`'s 1–4). A garbage value is rejected by the same rule whether it arrived over BLE or the sim seed — one source of truth, not a second guard on the wire.
- **Applied in the record task, L4.** The record task already owns both the `Recorder` and the `AlertEngine`, so `apply_settings` lives there, applied before the event mutates run totals — config can never disturb the recording math. The sim demo now flows through the *same* `apply_settings` (a demo `WatchSettings`) instead of a separate hardcoded seam, so the sim actually exercises the sync path.
- **Transport: a write-only BLE characteristic + a golden-vector-pinned Dart encoder.** The Threkir GATT service gains a fourth (`write`) characteristic that decodes the frame and publishes to `state::SETTINGS`; like the rest of the `ble` build it compiles + links but is hardware-unverified. The phone encoder is a Dart mirror pinned to the same golden byte vector as the Rust test (the run-sync discipline), so the two codecs can't drift.

The doubly-optional `zone_ceiling` (`Option<Option<u8>>`) lets a push both *set* (`Some(Some(z))`) and *clear* (`Some(None)`, wire byte 0) the ceiling, versus an absent field that leaves it alone — the phone needs to turn the alert off, not only change it.

## 218. Five more watch_core parity cores land as host-tested pure logic before any page wiring

**Decided (2026-07-11).** Five further shipped web helpers were ported to `no_std` `watch_core` modules — `age_grade` (web `runs/age_grade.ts` + the generated `age_grade_tables.ts`), `hydration` (`nutrition/hydration.ts`), `exercise_calories` (`nutrition/exercise_calories.ts`), `training_paces` (the pace-zone surface of `training/training.ts`), and `fitness` (`training/fitness.ts`) — each mirroring its web `.test.ts` test-for-test (12 / 9 / 11 / 10 / 46 = 88 new host tests, `watch_core` now 435 green). This continues the §215 / 2026-07-10 "logic before peripherals" cadence: the cores are built and host-verified first, and **run-view page wiring is a deliberate separate follow-up** — this round touched none of `face`/`page`/`record`/`ui` to avoid colliding with the three in-flight page-wiring/UI/settings PRs (§216, §217, glance-wiring), and the `//!` module list documents each as unwired.

Three port-shaping choices worth recording, all in service of §24 (the watch ports web logic, it does not pioneer): (a) `age_grade`'s 22×95×2 factor tables were emitted by a throwaway generator that parses `age_grade_tables.ts`, so the numbers are exact rather than hand-retyped (the same "never hand-edit the generated tables" discipline the web/Dart tables carry). (b) `training_paces` reuses the already-ported `race_predictor::riegel_predict` rather than re-porting Riegel, and ports the pace-zone surface **as it exists on web** — five Daniels zones derived from a *goal pace* (optionally via a recent-race Riegel projection) with the female (×1.03) and masters calibrations — not an invented VDOT→zone table the web doesn't have. (c) `fitness` reads `Run` rows keyed by an ISO `started_at` and `Date.now()`, neither of which belongs in a `no_std` firmware core; following `training_load`'s precedent it collapses both to an integer **day index**, and models `recovery_advice`'s output as an enum so no English strings live in the pure helper (the `badges`/`onboarding` pattern). These are one-way faithful ports host-tested with `cargo test`; they are **not** part of the enforced web↔mobile `shared-library-syncer` lockstep, and are **not** sim/hardware-verified (Renode tooling is absent on the dev machine). A live de-dup follow-up falls out of this round: now that `exercise_calories` is its own module, the private `runCalories` inlined in `roadbook`/`fuel_plan` can be replaced with it.
## 219. Ten more watch_core parity cores land as host-tested pure logic before any page wiring

**Decided (2026-07-11).** A second, larger batch of shipped web helpers was ported to `no_std` `watch_core` modules — `route_markers`, `checkpoint_projection`, `route_description`, `plan_progress`, `plan_adherence`, `current_week`, `nutrition_targets`, `challenge_progress`, `badges`, and `locale_defaults` — each mirroring its web `.test.ts` test-for-test (10 / 10 / 12 / 10 / 11 / 10 / 18 / 22 / 15 / 4 = 122 new host tests, `watch_core` now 469 green). This is the same cadence as §215 and §218: build and host-verify the pure cores first, and keep **run-view page wiring as a deliberate separate follow-up** — this batch touched none of `face`/`page`/`record`/`ui` so it can't collide with the in-flight page-wiring / UI-overhaul / settings-sync PRs. The selection was weighted to the watch's real domain (run / course / race / fuel / training) and deliberately skipped the off-domain gym / yoga / events helpers, which would be dead code on a running watch with no plausible wiring.

Because each worker built in an isolated worktree branched off `main`, every port reuses only symbols already on `main`, never another same-batch port: `checkpoint_projection` reuses `roadbook::CutoffStatus` + `cutoff_eta::CUTOFF_TIGHT_S` + `roadbook::MAX_ROADBOOK_LEGS`; `route_description` reuses `distance_bands::band_for_distance`; `challenge_progress` reuses `pacer::ON_PACE_BAND` (whose web-canonical home is `challenge_progress.ts`, but the watch already placed the constant in `pacer`, so the port imports it rather than redefining). `nutrition_budget` was dropped from the batch precisely because it depends on `nutrition_targets`, which was being built the same round. The recurring §24 port-shaping choices held: date/`nowMs` inputs collapse to an integer day index (`current_week` pins it to the Unix epoch so day-of-week is recoverable without a timezone DB); classifier outputs that are English strings on web become Rust enums (`plan_adherence`'s drift/advice; `badges`' i18n label keys are carried as identifiers, never English text — the thresholds/ids/tiers/icons remain the lockstep, consistent with the SQL awarder); and narrow-union string inputs become enums. `route_markers`' `parse_cutoff` was written to match the copy inlined in `roadbook`/`cutoff_eta`, so a later de-dup lands cleanly. These are one-way faithful ports host-tested with `cargo test`; they are **not** part of the enforced web↔mobile `shared-library-syncer` lockstep, and are **not** sim/hardware-verified (Renode tooling is absent on the dev machine).

## 220. A third watch_core parity batch ports the last domain-relevant helpers; off-domain helpers stay unported by design

**Decided (2026-07-11).** A third batch of shipped web helpers was ported to `no_std` `watch_core` modules — `privacy`, `segments`, `track_projection`, `run_heatmap`, `relink_candidates`, `live_freshness`, and `finisher_certificate` — each mirroring its web `.test.ts` test-for-test (8 / 29 / 4 / 9 / 10 / 5 / 5 = 70 new host tests, `watch_core` now 417 green), same §215 / §218 / §219 cadence: pure cores first, run-view page wiring a deliberate later follow-up, and none of `face`/`page`/`record`/`ui` touched so the batch can't collide with the in-flight page-wiring / UI-overhaul / settings-sync PRs.

This batch is explicitly the **curated tail**: with the high-value run / course / race helpers (GAP, cut-offs, roadbook, fuel, race predictor, pace segments, training load, course/nav, age grade, HR zones, training paces, fitness, route markers, checkpoint projection, route description) already ported across the first two batches, the parity list's remaining entries are dominated by **off-domain** surfaces — food-logging (`meal_template`, `recipe`, `nutrition_budget`, `nutrition_week`), gym programming, yoga session plans, event recurrence, and phone-only concerns (`recap`, `onboarding`, `rate_limit_errors`, `exif_strip`). Those were **deliberately left unported**: a standalone ultra-running watch has no plausible wiring for a food diary or a gym-superset editor, so porting them would add host-tested dead code and imply a feature surface the device does not have (against the spirit of § 24). The rule for future batches: port a helper when it plausibly serves the watch's run / course / race / fuel / training job, not merely because it exists on web. The usual §24 shaping choices recurred — `privacy`/`track_projection`/`run_heatmap`/`segments` model a local `TrackPoint` and reuse the crate's existing `grade_adjusted_pace::haversine_metres` where a great-circle distance is needed (rather than duplicating it); `relink_candidates` collapses ISO dates to an integer day index; `live_freshness` collapses its two timestamps to a monotonic age-in-seconds; `crownLabel` / the freshness bucket become enums, not English strings; `run_heatmap` and `finisher_certificate` port only the unit/locale-agnostic twin surface and leave the MapLibre GeoJSON emitters / SVG-PNG builder web-only. Not part of the enforced web↔mobile `shared-library-syncer` lockstep, and not sim/hardware-verified (Renode tooling absent on the dev machine).

## 221. Every `release-*.yml` deploy is triggered by publishing a GitHub Release, not a tag push

**Decided (2026-07-11).** All nine per-app release workflows moved from `on: push: tags: ['<app>@*']` to `on: release: [published]`. Previously a tag push ran the workflow, which then *created* a GitHub Release as an output — so the Release was a side effect, and the very first web release existed only after a deploy had already happened. Inverting it makes the published Release the **deploy gate and trigger**: `gh release create <app>@<version>` (or the Releases UI, or the `/release` skill) creates the tag and the Release in one deliberate, human-authored act, and that `release: published` event is what fires the deploy. A bare `git push origin <tag>` now deploys nothing.

Three consequences shaped the implementation: (a) `on: release` fires for *every* published Release in the repo, so each job is guarded with `if: startsWith(github.event.release.tag_name, '<app>@')` — publishing a `web@*` Release runs only `release-web`; the other eight evaluate false and skip. (b) The artifact-building workflows (android/iOS/Wear/web) keep a final softprops step but make it **attach-only** — no `name`/`generate_release_notes`, `tag_name: ${{ github.event.release.tag_name }}` — so the build output rides back onto the existing Release for rollback without clobbering the human-authored title/notes; the deploy-only workflows (backend/worker/osrm/graph-cycle) and the watchOS smoke-check drop their old "create an audit-record Release" step entirely (redundant now that the Release is the trigger), which also let their job-level `contents: write` be removed (least-privilege — they inherit top-level `contents: read`). (c) Version extraction is unchanged: `GITHUB_REF` is `refs/tags/<tag>` on a `release` event, so the existing `${GITHUB_REF#refs/tags/...}` parses and `release-web`'s `startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/web@')` environment selector still resolve correctly. This composes with the same-day `production` environment required-reviewer gate: a prod deploy now needs two deliberate acts — a published Release **and** an environment approval. Note that a Release created by another workflow's `GITHUB_TOKEN` does not chain-trigger these (GitHub's anti-recursion rule), which is fine because Releases here are cut by a human/`gh` token.

## 222. Google sign-in (web + mobile) is fail-closed behind a config gate, not deleted, until the provider is wired

**Decided (2026-07-11).** The `/login` "Continue with Google" button was disabled ahead of a web-only launch where the Supabase `google` provider isn't configured yet (a live provider would just surface an opaque OAuth error). Rather than delete the built — and GDPR-reviewed — Google path (`handleGoogleSignIn` + its consent-timestamp stashing for `/auth/callback`, `auth.signInWithGoogle`), it is gated behind the fail-closed `PUBLIC_GOOGLE_AUTH_ENABLED` flag (`apps/web/src/lib/core/google_auth_flag.ts`, mirroring `coach_flag.ts`). Off (the default: unset / empty / not-truthy) the button keeps its label but renders a "Soon" pill and its click shows the `login.googleSoon` notice — the same treatment as the not-yet-wired Apple button beside it. Re-enabling is a one-variable flip (`PUBLIC_GOOGLE_AUTH_ENABLED=true`) the day the provider is configured, with no re-review of the consent code. Local dev + e2e set it truthy in `.env.development`, so the smoke / i18n / signup-age-gate specs that read or click the button keep passing; prod (sops env, unset) shows the coming-soon state. Chosen over deletion because re-enablement is imminent and the consent path is security-sensitive; chosen over a hardcoded coming-soon because the working code should not be thrown away and the repo already leans on fail-closed public-env gates for exactly this shape (`PUBLIC_COACH_ENABLED`, `PUBLIC_ROUTE_GEN_ENABLED`).

On mobile the analogous gate is the **existing** `GOOGLE_WEB_CLIENT_ID` config signal, not a new flag: `sign_in_screen`/`sign_up_screen`'s `_signInWithGoogle` now checks it first and, when unset, shows the `googleSignInSoon` notice (on sign-up, before the age/terms gate — an unconfigured button shouldn't nag) instead of the old developer-facing "not configured" exception. The Flutter Google path itself is untouched and reactivates when the operator sets the client id. Also in the same change: the marketing landing page (`apps/web/src/routes/+page.svelte`) flipped Android and Wear OS to `comingSoon: true` (joining iOS + Apple Watch — web is the only launch platform), and the hero + watch-parity copy that claimed those apps "ship today" was corrected across all six locales.

## 223. The fundraising/donations UI is hidden behind a fail-closed client flag until Stripe + sign-off land

**Decided (2026-07-11).** The charity fundraising feature (§167) was already fail-closed server-side (Stripe keys unset → the donations-checkout / events-checkout Edge Functions 503), but its **UI still rendered unconditionally** — the public `/fundraisers/[id]` page, the donate flow, and the owner "Create fundraiser" CTA on run-detail + event-detail (`FundraiserSection`). Ahead of the web-only launch (no Stripe Connect keys, no owner/CISO/counsel sign-off yet), that meant users would see donate affordances that dead-end on the 503. Added `PUBLIC_FUNDRAISING_ENABLED` (`src/lib/social/fundraising_flag.ts`, mirroring `coach_flag.ts` / `google_auth_flag.ts` / §222): off by default → `FundraiserSection` renders nothing and never fetches, and the public fundraiser page short-circuits its load to the existing not-found state. This is the client mirror of the server-only Stripe gate (the browser can't see the Connect keys), the same shape as the Coach gate. Local dev + e2e set it truthy in `.env.development` so the `tests-e2e/fundraising/fundraiser.spec.ts` create-CTA + public-page flow still passes; prod leaves it unset. Chosen over deleting the built feature (it's shipped, test-mode-verified, and only pending credentials + sign-off) and over a server-only gate (which would leave a visibly broken donate button). The separate `/settings/upgrade` "Donate" button is an external GitHub Sponsors link — unrelated to this Stripe-gated feature — and was left working.

## 224. A parallel watch batch pairs two more parity cores with the first page-wiring, de-dups, and driver work

**Decided (2026-07-11).** A single parallel pass advanced the watch on several fronts at once rather than porting-only: two further shipped web helpers landed as `no_std` `watch_core` cores — `route_geometry` (web `routes/route_geometry.ts`) and `route_gpx` (web `routes/route_gpx.ts`) — each mirroring its web `.test.ts` test-for-test; two de-dup follow-ups anticipated in §218 / §219 were closed (`roadbook` now reuses `route_markers::parse_cutoff` instead of its inlined copy, and `fuel_plan` reuses `exercise_calories::run_calories`); and the first **run-view page wiring** shipped, adding `training_paces` and `fitness` as two display-only glance pages on the BTN3 cycle (16 → 18). Driver / render work rode along: `ublox_nmea` GSV parsing feeding `state::SATS`, calibrated ambient temperature from the `bmp581` driver, MAX86177 motion-artifact hardening, and a `sharp_mip` `draw_sparkline` framebuffer primitive plus a `watch_render` `MiniProfile` widget. A `sim/nmea/mountain_loop.nmea` fixture was added for the sim feed.

This keeps the watch on its established discipline — logic before peripherals, host-tested pure cores, additive display-only pages over phone-pushed summaries, honest inactive/empty states, and no flash-format change — so it still pioneers no product feature per §24. The de-dups are the payoff for §219's "written to match so a later de-dup lands cleanly" note. Host-tested with `cargo test`; **not** sim/hardware-verified (Renode tooling is absent on the dev machine).

## 225. A 15-task parallel pass ports twelve more watch_core cores and wires every one as a run-view glance page

**Decided (2026-07-11).** A 15-task parallel pass ported twelve more shipped web helpers to `no_std` `watch_core` cores — `recap`, `streaks`, `run_stats`, `pr_recency`, `race_day`, `plan_replan`, `readiness`, `goals`, `turn_cues`, `route_simplify`, `route_elevation`, and `auto_segment_effort` — each host-tested test-for-test against its web `.test.ts`, `no_std` / `heapless` / `libm`, with i18n labels carried as identifiers, ISO dates collapsed to an integer day index, and web-only halves dropped (the recurring §218–§220 shaping). It also added GSA (DOP + fix-mode) parsing to the `ublox_nmea` driver, wired the two named UI follow-ups (GSV satellites-in-view → the idle-face signal meter via `statusbar::bars_for_sats`; an elevation mini-profile glance page), and then wired **all twelve** new cores as BTN3 run-view glance pages — the cycle grew to ~31 — each phone-fed through a plausibility-guarded setter with an honest empty state until synced (the `set_fitness` / `set_training_goal_pace_s_per_km` precedent).

The owner chose full page-wiring over the leaner anti-clutter option, accepting a long BTN3 cycle. That is affordable here because every page is **display-only over a phone-pushed summary**: the watch computes nothing new and surfaces no data the phone didn't already send, so it pioneers no product feature (§24) and needs no schema or flash-format change. Host-tested with `cargo test`; **not** sim/hardware-verified (Renode tooling is absent on the dev machine).

## 226. A watch batch hardens the driver/sensor path and makes the GPS signal meter honest, rather than adding display surfaces

**Decided (2026-07-11).** After two batches that added `watch_core` cores and wired display pages, a six-task parallel pass deliberately turned to the real hardware path. The `ublox_nmea` driver gained GLL + VTG parsing (so a receiver configured for those sentences isn't silently dropped); `bmp581` gained a QNH-calibrated `altitude_from_pressure_m` + `set_sea_level_pa` + `read_altitude_m` (a proper pressure→altitude conversion with a settable reference); `max86177` gained off-wrist / poor-contact detection off its existing DC-baseline + AC-envelope state, gating the HR reading's `valid` flag so an off-wrist sensor reads honest "no HR" instead of a garbage BPM off ambient light. The idle-face GPS signal meter was made honest: the GSA fix type publishes to `state::FIX_QUALITY`, and `statusbar::bars_for_fix` gates the sat-count ladder on it — no fix reads 0 bars ("searching") even under a full sky, a 2D fix caps below a 3D lock. A `gps_dropout.nmea` sim fixture exercises the staleness→reacquire path, and `guided_runs` was ported (the scripted-run library + `(prev, now]` cue dispatcher; TTS/translation dropped, cue text as i18n identifiers).

The batch's worktrees forked from a base predating §224/§225, so integration was a genuine 3-way merge against `main` rather than a file copy: main's GSA parsing was kept and the GLL/VTG + fix-quality work layered on, while a duplicate `Gsa` variant and a divergent `bars_for_sats` ladder produced by a stale-base agent were discarded. The lesson recorded for future parallel rounds: verify each worktree's fork point before integrating, and merge (never copy) when it predates recent work. Host-tested with `cargo test` + the CI `clippy -D warnings` gate; **not** sim/hardware-verified (Renode tooling is absent on the dev machine).

## 227. A fifteen-task parallel watch batch adds driver features + a port and hardens the on-device wire formats, integrated by per-agent cherry-pick onto a moved `main`

**Decided (2026-07-11).** Continuing the §226 hardware-path thrust, a fifteen-agent parallel pass added driver features and swept the correctness-critical cores rather than adding display surfaces (which §24 anyway caps — the watch is display-additive and pioneers no product feature). Driver features: `ublox_nmea` ZDA (UTC date) + GST (position-error) parsing; `bmp581` configurable OSR/IIR/power-mode with an ultra-watch altitude default; `max86177` LED-current AGC + die-temperature; `sharp_mip` circle/ring/dashed/arc primitives; `watch_render` dial + compass widgets. One on-domain parity port (`plan_adaptive_replan`, reusing the ported `plan_replan`/`plan_adherence`) landed as a pure core with page-wiring deliberately deferred. The six private `haversine` copies were de-duped onto one canonical `pub` helper (behaviour-preserving). The hardening half found and fixed a real fail-closed bug — `settings::decode` accepted trailing bytes + unknown presence bits and returned `Some`, which the BLE consumer applied as real config — and added boundary tests to seven more cores without changing any wire-format golden vector. `watch_core` grew to 1004 host tests.

Two integration lessons, both consequences of the stale-fork + concurrent-session reality §226 first flagged. First, all fifteen worktrees forked from a base predating the recent `main`, so integration was a **per-agent 3-way cherry-pick onto live `main`** (not a merge of branches, not a copy): clean for the disjoint-file agents, with genuine conflicts only where the agent's feature and main's §226 work touched the same file (`ublox_nmea` ZDA/GST vs GLL/VTG; `bmp581` OSR/IIR tests vs QNH tests), each resolved to keep **both**. Second, a **concurrent session was landing overlapping `custom_watch` work on `main` during the run** — it had already committed an equivalent `heat_flat`/`switchback_descent` sim-fixture pair, so that task was dropped rather than clobber it. A separate commit cleared a clippy-1.97 `-D warnings` regression that had reddened `main` independently. A found-but-unfixed **web-twin bug** is on record: `live_cutoff_eta.ts`/`.dart` grade a `NaN` pace as "on pace"; the ported Rust `cutoff_eta` already rejects it, and a `Number.isFinite` guard on the canonical web/mobile surface is owed. Host-tested + `clippy -D warnings` green; **not** sim/hardware-verified (Renode tooling absent on the dev machine).

## 228. Blocked users are hidden inside shared clubs — the roster + post feed carry the same `is_blocked_either_way` guard every other social read has

**Decided (2026-07-12, persona-hunt woman-runner Critical).** A user block hid each party from the other everywhere in the social graph — follows, kudos, comments, DMs, segment leaderboards, profile lookups — except the two club-scoped SELECT surfaces (`club_members` roster, `club_posts` feed), which gated purely on shared club membership. A blocked harasser who shared any club with the victim kept a durable, block-proof channel to her name, avatar, activity, and posts. Migration `20270402_001` re-creates the current authoritative policies (the roster split by status in `20260926_001`, the feed last re-emitted in `20270113_001`) with `and not is_blocked_either_way(auth.uid(), <user_id|author_id>)` appended, and nothing else changed. Two carve-outs are deliberate and documented in the migration header: the "own membership" self-row (self is never blocked — `user_blocks` CHECKs `blocker_id <> blocked_id`), and the admin pending-request moderation queue (hiding a blocked requester would strand the request with nobody able to approve/reject it — block is a visibility primitive, not a moderation veto). Anon is unaffected (`is_blocked_either_way(NULL, …)` is false, so public rosters/posts stay readable logged-out). Pinned by `club_block_enforcement_test.sql`.

## 229. RevenueCat tier writes are monotonic on an event timestamp, so out-of-order delivery can't downgrade a paying user

**Decided (2026-07-12, backend bug-hunt Medium).** The webhook's insert-first `webhook_events` dedupe only rejects a replay of the *same* `event.id`; it does nothing against two *distinct* events arriving out of order, and the 7-day freshness window keeps a stale `EXPIRATION` "fresh" long after a newer `RENEWAL`. A network reorder (activation delivered before an older expiration) therefore silently downgraded a re-subscribed paying user to `free` until the next renewal. Migration `20270403_001` adds `user_profiles.tier_updated_event_ts` (epoch-ms, service-role-locked via the re-emitted `lock_subscription_columns()` — closing a self-freeze where a user could stamp a far-future ts on their own row and pin `pro` forever), and the webhook now applies any tier change with an atomic conditional UPDATE (`… where tier_updated_event_ts is null or tier_updated_event_ts <= <incoming>`). An out-of-order deactivation matches zero rows and no-ops; in-order lapses still downgrade. Chosen over re-deriving from RC's `GET /subscribers` on every deactivation (an extra network hop on the hot path). The `lifetime` shield and event-id dedupe are untouched. Pinned by the `revenuecat-webhook/lib.test.ts` monotonic cases.

## 230. A process-killed in-progress run is resumable — cold start re-hydrates and continues the same run rather than always finalizing it

**Decided (2026-07-12, Moab-240 persona Critical).** On cold start the app had only two outcomes for a non-empty `in_progress` partial: finalize it into a *finished* `Run` (`recovered`) or discard it. An OS process-kill mid-run (Doze/LMK over a multi-day ultra, an overnight sleep-station background reap) therefore split one continuous effort into two disjoint records — breaking front-half/back-half analysis, the aid-station lap sequence, and cutoff-buffer math. `RunRecorder.resumeSession(...)` now re-hydrates the persisted track, distance, prior elapsed (via a new `_elapsedOffset`), original `startedAt`, and restored laps (in-progress saves now persist `metadata.laps`, round-tripped by `lapsFromCanonicalJson`), then continues appending to the *same* NDJSON file — no full-track rewrite. `evaluateInProgressPartial` gained a `resumable` outcome, gated on `in_progress_saved_at` being within a 48 h window (covers a canyon dead-zone plus a nap; stale → finalize, the safe default; no timestamp → finalize). Cold start with a resumable partial jumps to the run screen and offers **Resume** (primary), **Finish now** (finalize as-is), **Discard**. Elapsed deliberately counts `partial.duration + post-resume stopwatch`, *not* the dead-process gap, keeping the recorder's monotonic-clock honesty rather than inventing wall-clock time the phone may have spent idle. Pinned across `run_recorder_test.dart`, `in_progress_recovery_test.dart`, and `run_screen_recording_flow_test.dart`; iOS twin mirrored.

## 231. Challenge distance/duration goals exclude DNF efforts, matching the PR and achievements engines

**Decided (2026-07-12, backend bug-hunt).** `challenge_leaderboard` and `recompute_challenge_completion` summed a runner's in-window runs with no DNF filter, so a DNF'd ultra (42 km before dropping) still banked 42 km toward a "run 100 km this month" goal and its leaderboard value — inconsistent with the PR refresher and the achievements awarder, which both exclude DNF. Migration `20270407_001` re-emits both functions' latest bodies adding `and r.is_dnf = false` to every run scan (so the exclusion applies uniformly across distance/duration/vert/count/streak metrics). Chosen over counting DNF distance ("distance you actually covered") because consistency with the rest of the backend's DNF handling is the durable, least-surprising contract. Known bounded gap: the client twin `challenge_progress.ts` / `.dart` reads the `activities` view summary, which carries no `is_dnf`, so an offline optimistic estimate can still count a just-DNF'd run's distance until the next authoritative server re-fetch. The durable follow-up is to project `is_dnf` into the `activities` view runs-branch summary and filter it in the `metricFromActivity` twin — tracked, not yet done.

## 232. The public-schema role grant matrix is version-controlled, not left to Supabase's implicit default privileges

**Decided (2026-07-13, prod onboarding incident).** Onboarding failed in prod with `42501 permission denied for table user_profiles` (the profile PATCH) and the same on `user_settings` (a `?select=prefs` read) — the `authenticated` role had lost base table privileges. Root cause: the DML grants (`insert`/`update`/`delete`) and several table `SELECT`s were **never expressed in a migration**. They existed only via Supabase's default privileges (`grant all on all tables … to anon, authenticated, service_role`), which the column-lockdown migrations (`20260707_001`, `20260810_001`, clubs/events/checkpoint_crossings, coach_messages, event_attendees, challenge_participants) only ever *narrowed* on a handful of tables — the broad DML surface stayed implicit. Once prod drifted (restore / manual revoke / default-privileges regression) nothing in version control brought it back. Same `permission denied for table` class the `20260817_001` revert already hit once for views; §117's api_database note flagged the view side of this exact fragility. Migration `20270408_001` makes the intended matrix explicit and idempotent: one `grant` per (table, grantee) for the four app-relevant privileges (`select/insert/update/delete`) across all 89 public base tables, plus the column carve-outs (`user_profiles`/`clubs`/`events`/`checkpoint_crossings` SELECT, `coach_messages`/`challenge_participants`/`event_attendees` UPDATE); `app_quota` + `deletion_audit_log` stay service_role-only. Generated from the canonical fully-migrated schema and **additive only** — replaying it can bring a role up to the intended surface but never tightens one or grants table-level SELECT on a locked table, so it is a verified no-op on a healthy DB (RLS remains the row-level gate). Verified by reproduction: revoking the two privileges reproduces the exact 403s, applying the migration restores 204/200.

## 233. Segments gain a free-standing global/famous-segment catalogue that matches a run end-to-end without a linked route

**Decided (2026-07-13, runner-strava-migration persona Medium).** v1 segments (`20260526_001`) are slices of a *saved route* — a `segments` row references `routes(id)`, so a segment can only exist once someone re-created that stretch of road as an in-app route first. That's a chicken-and-egg gap for a fresh market with zero routes, and it means a migrating Strava user's imported runs (which land with `route_id` null) match nothing. Migration `20270411_001` adds a FREE-STANDING catalogue: `global_segments` carries its OWN polyline geometry (world-readable; only the `app_admins` allow-list may curate) plus a parallel `global_segment_efforts` table under the same run-privacy rules `segment_efforts` already honour (`private.is_run_visible_to` in the base-table RLS; `is_blocked_either_way` additionally in the leaderboard RPC). Matching is deliberately narrow — `computeGlobalSegmentEffort` (the `segments` TS↔Dart parity pair) scores a run END-TO-END against a CURATED geometry (pass near the start, then the end, having covered ~the segment length between, 35 m tolerance + a 25 %-of-length end-to-end guard), reusing the proven `computeEffortFromTrack` interpolation rather than inventing an arbitrary-geometry HMM/Hausdorff pipeline (that stays deferred). The web surfaces are the run-detail "Famous segments" chips (owner-only backfill on mount, RLS-gated write) and a new `/segments/[id]` detail page (public catalogue metadata + a block-guarded, gender/age-filterable leaderboard; the page renders only the segment's own public geometry and per-effort times/ranks — never another athlete's GPS track). Crucially, run-detail now mounts `RunSegmentEfforts` even when `route_id` is null (RouteHistory stays route-scoped), so the catalogue actually reaches the route-less imported runs it was built for. Pinned by `global-segment-catalogue-journey.spec.ts` (route-less run → chip → detail leaderboard) plus the `segments.test.ts` `global:` matcher cases and the backend `global_segments_test.sql` pgtap suite.

## 234. Cycle- and pregnancy-aware plan adjustment is a conservative, opt-in, consent- and flag-gated layer over the existing plan generator

**Decided (2026-07-13, persona-hunt woman-runner Medium).** The plan engine had no menstrual-cycle or pregnancy awareness, and no first-class pause/resume. Rather than a new intensity model, the feature is a bounded PATCH layer (`training/cycle_plan.ts` ↔ `cycle_plan.dart`, a TS↔Dart parity pair, 22 mirror tests each) that reuses the recovery-week ease shape.

**Exact algorithm + bounds (the reviewable design decision):**
- **Menstrual mode.** Phase is derived per workout DATE, not per week: day-in-cycle = `(date − last_period_start) mod cycle_length`; ovulation estimated as `cycle_length − 14` (the luteal phase is the stable one), menstruation = first 5 days, ovulatory = ovulation ±1, late-luteal = last 3 days. Only **menstrual + late-luteal** days are eased; follicular/ovulatory/early-luteal run exactly as prescribed. Ease = a **modest 15%** volume trim (`CYCLE_EASE_SCALE = 0.85`, deliberately NOT the 40% of a full recovery week) plus clearing the strict pace target on quality sessions so they are run by feel (kind unchanged). `rest` and the goal `race` are never touched. Cycle lengths outside 21–40 days are refused (no adjustment) — fail-safe.
- **Pregnancy mode.** Trimester is derived by counting back 40 weeks from the due date (T1 <14w, T2 14–27w, T3 ≥28w); dates outside the pregnancy are left unadjusted. Within it, every quality session AND the race are stripped to easy, effort-based running (no intervals, no strict pace, `structure` cleared) and all distances taper progressively — T1 ×0.9, T2 ×0.75, T3 ×0.5. An explicit “not medical advice — consult your provider” disclaimer is shown in the settings input and the apply confirm.
- Adjustments apply to **future workouts only** (today onward); the past is not rewritten. Touched weeks’ stated volume is recomputed from the patched distances.

**Sensitivity gating (Art 9 reproductive-health data, fail-closed).** The inputs (mode + cycle length + last-period-start + due date) live in `user_settings.prefs`, written on `/settings/account` ONLY when the existing explicit health-data consent (`health_data_consent_at`) is granted — nulled on withdrawal, same as `date_of_birth`. The WHOLE surface is additionally behind a fail-closed feature flag `PUBLIC_CYCLE_PLANS_ENABLED` (default off; enable with the literal `true`/`1`/`yes`/`on`) so it cannot reach users until owner + CISO + counsel sign-off flips it at deploy time. **Pre-deploy checklist item, not a blocked-on-legal TODO:** the code path is complete and tested behind the two gates; going live is the sign-off.

**Pause/resume.** A first-class `paused` plan status (migration `20270412_001`, CHECK widened) with `pausePlan`/`resumePlan` helpers. Pausing frees the `training_plans_one_active` slot; resuming refuses (`ActivePlanExistsError`) when another plan is already active rather than silently completing it. Mobile UI + mobile pause/resume + the mobile cycle-adjust screen are DEFERRED (additive per §24); the pure helper is twinned now.

## 235. Solo runs recorded after dark with no live share get a one-time, throttled, dismissible nudge — the pure decision is twinned, the banner is a mobile-only L4 surface

**Decided (2026-07-12, persona-hunt woman-runner High).** The auto-live-share + overdue-escalation net (§207) only watches *live-broadcast* runs; a runner who never enabled `auto_live_share` (default off) and never tapped "Share live link" got no net and — the actual finding — no *signal* that she was recording unprotected. The fix is a one-time contextual nudge at run start, not an expansion of the escalation into a duration-based net (rejected: telemetry silence is still the only honest overdue signal, and a no-ping run has none). `shouldNudgeSoloSafety` (pure TS↔Dart twin `safety/safety_nudge.ts` ↔ `safety_nudge.dart`, 14 mirror tests each) fires only when unprotected AND after dark AND not throttled — `autoLiveShareOn` off, `isBroadcast` false, `nudgeDismissed` false, `isNightWindow(nowLocalMinutes)` true. Night is a **fixed 20:00–06:00 local window**, deliberately not astronomical sunrise/sunset: no new astronomy dependency, deterministic, and nudging slightly early in summer is harmless where silently missing a dark run is not. Throttled 30 days via the device pref `safety_nudge_dismissed_at`, stamped when the transient/dismissible banner surfaces. The surface is `run_screen._maybeShowSafetyNudge()` inside `_attachRecordingSideEffects` (the complement of the auto-share block), wrapped in its own try/catch — L4, a failed daylight/pref read can never touch L0–L1 recording. Its action shares the *current run's* live link (one-off; it does NOT flip the public-by-default `auto_live_share` pref), the least-friction in-context close. Gated on a settings service being present so the throttle can persist (no service → no nudge, else it would nag every run — the friction the persona explicitly rejects). Recording is mobile-only, so per §24 the decision helper is the canonical twinned logic and the banner is the platform-additive surface — no web surface. An off-route → auto-notify tie-in stays deferred (no off-route-detection hook exists). iOS twin mirrored byte-for-byte. **Amendment (2026-07-17, issue #265):** the fixed 20:00–06:00 window silently missed genuinely-dark pre-dawn winter runs at higher latitudes (sunrise ~09:00 at 60°N in December). The window is now the FLOOR only: `isDarkOutside` returns the fixed window OR a seasonal `isSunDown(nowLocalMinutes, latitude, dayOfYear)` sunrise/sunset test (solar declination + latitude, solar noon assumed 12:00; longitude/EoT/DST error — ~1 h — is absorbed because the seasonal test only ADDS darkness, never narrows the window). `SoloSafetyNudgeInput` gains an optional `latitude`+`dayOfYear`; null (no GPS fix) degrades to exactly the old fixed window, so behaviour never regresses. The run screen feeds `_currentPosition?.lat` + the local day-of-year. Still no astronomy dependency (a few lines of trig); 24 mirror tests each. iOS twin mirrored byte-for-byte.

## 240. The overdue safety net gains an SMS leg + a per-run "not back by X" override — reversing two §207 open decisions

**Decided (2026-07-13, feature C "Trusted-contact SMS escalation").** ADR number deliberately skips ahead (230 → 240) to avoid colliding with sibling worktrees taking the next few numbers concurrently. §207 shipped the overdue net as email-only and left two things open ("no SMS/push channel yet"; "no per-run expected-return input"). This feature closes both, reusing the channel-agnostic scan rather than forking it. Migration `20270410_001` adds `safety_contacts.contact_phone` (nullable E.164, `~ '^\+[1-9][0-9]{6,14}$'` CHECK) + `safety_contacts.sms_opt_in_at`, and a `safety_sms` `jobs` kind. A contact's phone is **owner-stored**, but SMS is a **second, independent opt-in the contact controls**: `confirm_safety_contact(_by_token)` gained a defaulted `p_sms_opt_in` that only stamps `sms_opt_in_at` when a phone is on file (the RPC forces it null otherwise, so the anon token page can offer the box without knowing whether a number exists), and `set_safety_sms_opt_in` toggles it later. The overdue scan enqueues a `safety_sms` job **only** for a confirmed contact with `sms_opt_in_at` set — email is unconditional, SMS is additive, so a failed/disabled SMS leg never suppresses the email. The Go transport is **fail-closed**: `smsSender` stays nil unless `SMS_PROVIDER=twilio` **and** all three `TWILIO_*` vars are set, and the handler no-ops (logs, returns success) when nil — obtaining the Twilio account is a pre-deploy checklist item, not a code gate, exactly like the §207 CISO/counsel review. `set_run_expected_return(p_run_id, p_expected_return_at)` writes/clears `runs.metadata.expected_return_at` on an owned in-progress run, giving the scan a per-run absolute deadline layered over the silence-window pref (documented in metadata.md). Web surfaces: an optional phone on the `/settings/safety` add form (client-validated against the same CHECK), an "SMS on" badge, the contact-side opt-in checkbox on inbound requests + the `/safety/confirm` token page (now prompt-then-confirm rather than auto-confirm), and an owner-only "Not back by X" control on `/live/[id]` (web can't record, so the owner's live spectator view is the only in-progress-run surface). Consent posture is unchanged in shape — both sides opt in, payload carries times + link only, never coordinates. Mobile safety-contacts screens don't yet carry the phone/SMS fields; additive per §24, tracked as a follow-up.

## 241. Off-route → auto-notify trusted contact — client-detected, escalated through the existing notify path, prod-gated by a flag

**Decided (2026-07-13, feature D "Off-route auto-notify").** §207 (email overdue net) and §240 (SMS + per-run deadline) both trigger on telemetry *silence*. This closes the last leg of the persona-woman safety cluster — the runner who is still moving and still pinging but has left, and stayed off, their planned route. §235's solo-run nudge explicitly deferred this ("no off-route-detection hook exists yet"); it now exists. **Detection is client-side and twinned:** only the recording run screen computes off-route distance vs the planned route, so `OffRouteAlertDetector` (`apps/web/src/lib/safety/off_route_alert.ts` ↔ `apps/mobile_android/lib/off_route_alert.dart`, a TS↔Dart parity pair) is a pure debounce — fires once after > 75 m off route for ≥ 90 s continuous (a single GPS spike / brief corner-cut resets the clock; the 75 m is deliberately larger than the 40 m off-route *banner* threshold so a wobble doesn't escalate), latching for the run's lifetime. **Escalation reuses, does not fork, the notify path:** migration `20270414_001` adds `escalate_run_off_route(p_run_id)`, a SECURITY DEFINER RPC that enqueues the SAME `safety_email` (+ additive `safety_sms`) jobs the overdue scan does, with a distinct `off_route` template, and shares the once-per-run `metadata.safety_escalated_at` stamp — so a runner gets ONE alert per run whichever trigger fires first (§207 decision 4 holds across both triggers). No new `jobs.kind`, no `public_runs` change, no new metadata key. **Fail-closed, defence in depth:** the client calls only when the `OFF_ROUTE_ESCALATION_ENABLED` deploy flag is on (a `*_flag.ts`/dotenv gate, default off, the CISO/counsel deploy gate), the `safety_off_route_alerts` U pref is on (default off), a route is selected, AND a live broadcast is active (so the `/live` link the contact receives works); the RPC independently re-checks owner-only, in-progress-stub-only, the opt-in pref, ≥ 1 confirmed contact, and the atomic once-per-run stamp. The SMS leg additionally needs the default-unset Twilio provider (§240). Following "compliance sign-offs gate prod, not code": the whole path is written, tested, and landed; the sign-off is a pre-deploy checklist item (review the `off_route` copy + posture, then flip the flag), not a stubbed feature. Web carries only the opt-in toggle (no recording surface); the detector + trigger + banner are mobile, with the iOS byte-identical twin.
## 242. Route-builder OSRM calls go through a server-side proxy on web; mobile keeps calling the self-hosted engine directly

**Decided (2026-07-14, issue #198 — persona-hunt route-builder review).** The web route builder's manual waypoint snapping/routing fetched the OSRM host **directly from the browser** over `PUBLIC_OSRM_URL`, so a user's pin coordinates (the first pin is routinely their home) plus client IP left the client with no server boundary — the exact exposure the GraphHopper hop was built to prevent on the generate path, making deployment.md's "privacy parity" claim aspirational for the builder. Web now routes every OSRM call through `/api/routes/osrm/*` — a transport-agnostic core (`$lib/routes/osrm_proxy/handler.ts`) wrapped by a dev-only SvelteKit route and a production Lambda (`apps/web/lambda/osrm-proxy/`), mirroring generate-route's two-wrapper shape — and the OSRM base is the server-only `OSRM_URL` (never `PUBLIC_`). The proxy mirrors OSRM's own GET path shape so client parsing and the path-matched Playwright mocks carried over unchanged; it validates + rebuilds every upstream URL (enumerated services/profiles, range-checked coords, allowlisted query params — no open relay), requires a signed-in Supabase user, and fail-closes: unset `OSRM_URL` → 501 (builder degrades to straight-line segments); the demo fallback to `router.project-osrm.org` survives **only** in the dev wrapper (NODE_ENV-gated), never the Lambda — the posture `assertOsrmConfiguredForProd` enforced client-side now lives where the config does, pinned by `security_guards.test.ts`. **Mobile twin assessed and deliberately left direct:** `routing.dart` already reads a server-only-style `OSRM_URL` from bundled dotenv with a release-mode guard against the demo endpoint, so a native build talks TLS-direct to our self-hosted engine only — there is no client-visible bundle a third party can read the URL out of, no third-party page context, and no browser network tab; inserting the Lambda hop for mobile would add a cross-region round trip + auth overhead per snap without changing who receives the coordinates (our infra either way). Revisit only if the OSRM host ever moves to a third party (then mobile must ride the proxy too).

## 243. Legal pages carry complete text; only operator facts stay pending, behind a config seam

**Decided (2026-07-14, legal-pages completion round).** The public legal pages (`/privacy`, `/terms`, `/cookie-notice`, plus the new `/health-data-notice`) were rewritten from counsel-unreviewed drafts into complete legal text, synced against the compliance registries (`docs/compliance/sub-processors.md`, `retention.md`, `data-subject-rights.md`) and verified by three independent reviews (the `runner-privacy-conscious` persona for factual drift, `us-legal-doc-reviewer`, `intl-legal-doc-reviewer` — findings in `reviews/`). Drift fixed: 48h (not 24h) live-ping retention + the `race_pings` sibling; direct messages, checkpoint medical weigh-ins (Art 9, 90-day scrub), body metrics/food/gym, privacy zones, safety contacts, and push tokens added as disclosed categories; Open Food Facts + Upstash Redis added to the sub-processor list; GPC disclosed as honoured (the notice previously implied the opposite); the Terms gained marketplace terms (host as merchant of record, per-event refund policy, donations-go-to-the-owner honesty), DMCA notice-and-takedown, indemnification, an Art 22 statement lives in the policy, and the EU 14-day withdrawal clause was reclassified from digital-content full-waiver to digital-service pro-rata mechanics. The **loud "Draft — do not rely on it" banners are gone**; the genuinely unknowable operator facts (registered postal address, governing-law state, Art 27 EU/UK representatives) live in `apps/web/src/lib/legal/operator.ts` as nullable constants — each `null` renders a clearly-marked "pending" line, so filling a fact is a one-line config edit, per §150's gate-lives-in-config rule. Counsel sign-off + those facts + registering the DMCA agent + creating the `dmca@threkir.com` alias are pre-deploy checklist items in `docs/ops/deployment.md`, not code gaps. Pinned by `tests-e2e/legal/pages.spec.ts` (anon render, no draft markers, 48h figure, marketplace + DMCA presence, GPC disclosure, health-notice link). Legal pages stay English-by-design (single authoritative language; the followups RTL note already records this).

## 244. Every public-schema function pins search_path; the eight security-definer views are accepted advisor findings, by design

**Decided (2026-07-14, Supabase security-advisor sweep).** The prod dashboard's security advisor raised two lint categories. (1) *Function Search Path Mutable* — 28 public functions (trigger functions, the three trgm/postgis search helpers, small stable helpers; none SECURITY DEFINER, every definer function already pinned per house convention) resolved unqualified references against the caller's mutable session search_path. Migration `20270415_001` pins them via `ALTER FUNCTION … SET search_path` — deliberately not `create or replace`, so the bare-body-strips-prior-fixes trap can't fire; it is also deliberately numbered AFTER `20270410_001`, whose unpinned re-emission of `safety_contacts_force_unconfirmed()` would otherwise wipe the pin at replay (`create or replace` resets proconfig). The four that call postgis objects (`routes_set_geom`, `search_clubs`, `search_public_events`, `search_race_listings`) pin `public, extensions` (postgis lives in `extensions` on Supabase); the rest pin `public`. `function_search_path_test.sql` is a pg_proc catch-all so an unpinned function fails the pgtap suite instead of resurfacing in the advisor. (2) *Security Definer View* — the eight `public_*` views are flagged CRITICAL but are **intentional and stay definer**: they are the column-/metadata-key-redaction layer (§33, §201) and the *only* public read path — the base tables' anyone-can-read policies were dropped when clients switched to the views, so `security_invoker = on` wouldn't "fix" them, it would blank every share page and feed; restoring base-table anon SELECT to compensate would re-expose exactly the columns the views exist to redact (RLS is row-level, not column-level). Their write surface is already locked (§201's revoke-then-grant + `view_write_privileges_test.sql`), each pre-filters `is_public = true`. These eight findings are accepted permanently; re-judge against this entry if the advisor list changes shape.

## 245. RLS policies hoist auth.* into InitPlans and every FK carries a covering index; the multiple-permissive-policies advisor warnings are accepted

**Decided (2026-07-14, Supabase performance-advisor sweep — the Security sweep's sibling, see §244).** Three advisor categories, two fixed, one accepted. (1) *Auth RLS InitPlan* — 261 policies (244 public + 17 storage) called `auth.uid()` / `auth.jwt()` / `auth.role()` (once, `current_setting()`) bare in their expressions, re-evaluating the JWT parse for every row scanned; on list endpoints that's thousands of per-row calls doing identical work. Migration `20270416_001` wraps every bare call as `(select auth.uid())` so the planner hoists it into a once-per-statement InitPlan — semantically identical (the functions are STABLE and constant within a statement), generated mechanically from the deparsed `pg_policies` quals, and applied with `ALTER POLICY`, which only replaces the named clause: names, roles, and cmd stay put, so the drop-policy-wrong-name trap can't fire and no sibling policy can be lost. `rls_initplan_test.sql` is the pg_policies catch-all that keeps future policies wrapped. (2) *Unindexed foreign keys* — 41 FKs had no covering index, so every parent delete/update seq-scanned the child table; the delete-account cascade pays this on every child of `auth.users` (`live_run_pings`, `race_pings`, `notifications`, …). Migration `20270417_001` adds the 41 covering indexes (existing `<table>_<column>` naming); `fk_covering_index_test.sql` keeps future FKs indexed. (3) *Multiple permissive policies* — 59 table/role/action combos carry more than one permissive policy by deliberate design: this repo documents intent through separately-named policies ("users own their runs" + "active coach reads athlete runs"), and merging them into OR-blobs would trade a marginal per-row saving for exactly the bare-body/policy-name traps the migration history keeps hitting. Accepted permanently, like §244's definer views. The *unused index* lint is judgeable only against prod query stats — re-check it in the dashboard once real traffic has accumulated; local replay can't see it.
## 246. CodeQL runs on every commit — no docs paths-ignore, no cancellation of in-flight main analyses

**Decided (2026-07-14, Scorecard code-scanning alert 162).** Scorecard's SAST check requires a completed SAST run on each of the last 30 default-branch commits; two things in `security.yml` broke that. First, the `paths-ignore: ['**/*.md', 'docs/**']` on both triggers meant a docs-only merge produced a main commit with zero CodeQL check runs (commit `a5ecdac5` was the concrete miss). Second, `concurrency: cancel-in-progress: true` grouped per-ref meant back-to-back merges cancelled the older commit's analysis — the pattern that produced a dozen cancelled runs in the old `codeql.yml`'s history. Both are removed: the workflow now triggers on every push/PR regardless of paths, and the concurrency group is per-SHA on pushes to main (nothing on main is ever cancelled) while PR pushes keep cancelling their superseded head. The trade-off is accepted CI cost — four CodeQL legs + Trivy (~10 runner-minutes) on docs-only commits and on each rapid-fire merge that used to be cancelled — in exchange for a 10/10 Scorecard SAST score and the guarantee that no commit lands unanalysed. The required "CI gate" merge check is untouched; docs-only PRs still merge on the docs gate without waiting for CodeQL.

## 247. /delete-account is a public instructions page, not a second deletion mechanism

**Decided (2026-07-14, Play Console data-safety round).** Google Play's Data safety form requires a "Delete account URL" that a user (or reviewer) can open without the app installed; pointing it at `/settings/account` fails the logged-out case — an auth-guarded client page renders nothing useful to someone who already uninstalled. The fix is a public, prerendered `/delete-account` page in the legal-pages family (same `.legal-page` shape, English-by-design, anon-allowed in `+layout.svelte`): step-by-step self-service path (web + mobile, linking `/settings/account` which round-trips through sign-in via `return_to`), an email fallback (`privacy@threkir.com`) for users who can no longer sign in, the deleted-data scope, and the retention carve-outs (28-day backup roll-off, hashed deletion-audit record, processor-held transaction records) mirrored from `/privacy` §7 so the two pages can't disagree. Deliberately **not** a self-service deletion form for logged-out users — identity on an email request is verified manually, and the actual erasure stays the single audited `delete-account` Edge Function path. Pinned by `tests-e2e/legal/pages.spec.ts` (anon render, settings link, mailto fallback, 28-day figure, the /privacy cross-link). The Play/App Store console entry is checklist item 6 in `docs/ops/deployment.md`.

## 248. Health-data consent withdrawal is a SECURITY DEFINER RPC; writes to client-provisioned singleton rows are row-count-verified

**Decided (2026-07-15, issue #233).** The GDPR Art 7(3) withdrawal paths could silently no-op while the UI confirmed success, for two stacked reasons. First, several ApiClient consent methods opened with `if (uid == null) return;` — an expired session made the "withdrawal" a client-side no-op the caller then celebrated; those methods now throw (`StateError('not signed in')`), matching the in-file `recordBodyWeightKg` precedent. Second, `user_profiles` rows are client-provisioned (no signup trigger), so any `update ... eq(id, uid)` against a user whose row doesn't exist matches 0 rows and reports success. The obvious fix — client upsert — is **impossible for the health columns**: PostgREST's `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` reads `excluded.<col>`, which requires SELECT privilege on the column, and SELECT on the non-public-safe columns is deliberately revoked (20260707_001/20270408_001), so the upsert 42501s. Hence two patterns, by column class: (a) the consent paths moved server-side — `withdraw_health_data_consent()` (migration 20270418_001, mirroring `withdraw_coach_consent()`) nulls the stamp + height/gender/DOB and erases the `body_metrics` series in one transaction (closing the partial-failure window between the old two client calls), and `grant_health_data_consent()` was re-emitted insert-or-update so a grant that beats the profile bootstrap still stamps; (b) plain profile-field saves (display name, parkrun number, height) do a row-count-verified `update(...).select('id')` and fall back to a plain `insert` on 0 rows — insert is fully granted, and RETURNING only needs the granted `id`. Callers on both platforms flip local state only after the server confirms. Pinned by `withdraw_health_data_consent_test.sql` (pgtap, 10 tests incl. the no-profile-row grant + withdrawal), `consent_write_guards_test.dart` (signed-out throws), and `consent_withdrawal_integration_test.dart` (wire-level round-trip on a fresh no-profile-row user).

## 249. The settings push is an upsert — the offline queue only catches throws, and a 0-row update never throws

**Decided (2026-07-15, issue #234).** Same root cause as §248, different surface: `user_settings` / `user_device_settings` rows are client-provisioned, and both platforms' settings push did read-merge-**update**. Against a missing row (a write before any successful load — an explicitly supported path, and the auto-provision write itself was un-error-checked) the update matched 0 rows and reported success, so the pending queue — which only fires on throw — never engaged, the local cache was stamped as pushed, and the next successful load overwrote local state with the server's `{}`. The bag carries `privacy_zones` and `safety_overdue_minutes`, so the silent revert was a privacy/safety failure. Both pushes (web `settings.ts` `pushUniversal`/`pushDevice`, mobile `settings_service.dart` `_pushUniversal`/`_pushDevice`) are now upserts carrying the merged bag (the device arm includes the NOT NULL `platform`), the auto-provision writes are error-checked (mobile's also moved to `ignoreDuplicates` upserts so concurrent loads can't 409 into the offline path), and `SettingsService` gained a `withClient` test seam mirroring `ApiClient.withClient`. Unlike the §248 health columns, these tables have full column SELECT grants, so a client upsert is viable here. Pinned by `settings_push_integration_test.dart` (a fresh no-row user's push must land the row) and the web source guard `settings_push_guard.test.ts`.

## 250. Local routes get the §67 treatment — a device-local owner-tag sidecar, filtered getters, and push-time adoption

**Decided (2026-07-15, issue #229).** `LocalRouteStore` leaked across accounts three ways: it survived sign-out (deliberately — an unsynced route built offline is worth preserving, the same rationale §67 used for runs), had no owner concept, and both its "My routes" read seam and the sync drain served every row to whichever account was active — so user A's home-anchored route library rendered for user B and A's unsynced routes became B's cloud rows on B's first drain. Wiping at sign-out (the simpler alternative) was rejected for the same reason §67 rejected it for runs: it destroys a signed-in user's unsynced offline work. Instead the store carries a third sidecar, `route_owner_tags.json`, mapping route id → the ACCOUNT that saved it ('' = saved signed out). This is deliberately distinct from `Route.userId`, which names the route's *creator* — a bookmarked community route carries someone else's `userId` but belongs to whoever bookmarked it, so creator-based filtering would break bookmarks. The store stamps the tag on every `save`/`saveBatch` from a `currentUserIdProvider` (`() => api?.userId`, the same idiom §67 wired into `LocalRunStore`), and `routes` / `unsyncedRoutes` / `offlinePinnedRoutes` filter to tag == active-account ∪ untagged. Untagged rows (pre-upgrade files, signed-out builds) stay visible to every account and are adopted on push — `SyncService._drainUnsyncedRoutes` calls `tagRoutesOwner(succeededIds, api.userId)` after a successful drain, mirroring §67's null-owner adoption. Last-account-to-save wins on tag collisions (two accounts bookmarking the same public route re-tag it on their own hydrate, self-healing). Pinned by the `owner tags` group in `local_route_store_test.dart` (6 tests).

## 251. Sign-out resets the bag-mirrored Preferences, clears the runs delta watermark, and drops the departing account's cached bags

**Decided (2026-07-15, issue #231).** The sign-in overlay (`SettingsSyncService._applyUniversal`) is deliberately conditional on key presence — it only overwrites a local mirror when the incoming bag carries the key. That is correct for the online merge, but it meant a key ABSENT from user B's bag kept user A's value on the device indefinitely: A's `privacy_default = public` made B's new runs save public, A's body weight drove B's calorie estimates, A's goals rendered as B's (and B's first goal edit pushed the A-derived value into B's server bag), plus fueling rates, default activity, verbosity, and units.
